conversions to the reference structures of the objects are inserted and cancelled as in the
proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:gauge-functor}. Finally, the filling has
exactly one index-zero solution, with limit $q_*$ and invertible
operator (Paper~I, Proposition~9.4), so $\theta_G=\pm o_{q_*}$.
Paper~I, Proposition~9.5, and \cite[Proposition~5.4]{KM-unknot} are
stated on homology. The composition holds at chain level, up to an
integral chain homotopy, by the stretching formula
\cite[Section~3.9, (23)]{KM-unknot}, with the conversions treated as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:gauge-functor}; so the identity holds up to chain
homotopy.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Two-handle square]\label{thm:two-handle}
Let $W_2\colon M_0\to M_1$ be a two-handle cobordism with the data of
Section~\ref{subsec:two-handle-data}, and let $p_0$ and $p_1$ be the
presentations of $M_0$ and $M_1$ given by the pairs $(P_0,P_1)$ and
$(P_0,P_2)$, transported to the global frames as in Paper~I,
Section~9.8. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:twohandle}
 N_{p_1}\IG(W_2)\simeq\pm S_{W_2}N_{p_0}\qquad\text{in }\cK(\ZZ).
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The frame transports of Paper~I, Theorem~9.13, are pushforwards under
diffeomorphisms and bundle isomorphisms preserving the torus decoration;
their mixed squares are those of Section~\ref{sec:descent}. Evaluate the
second input of~\eqref{eq:triangle-square} on the cycle $\theta_G$, and
apply Lemma~\ref{lem:gauge-triangle-Z}. Since $N_{12}\theta_G=\theta_S$,
this gives~\eqref{eq:twohandle}. Changing the reference structures
conjugates this identity by the right-placed conversions and
uses~\eqref{eq:compose-homotopy}. Twisting the bundle on the two-handle
changes the gauge triangle class but not the orientation argument; its
relative bundle is retained throughout the mixed family.
\end{proof}

For simultaneous gluing at two triangle ends, use
one stabilization off both ends. The determinant
comparison is independent of the order of gluing,
up to the graded reordering already present in
\eqref{eq:tensor-maps}. Thus the triangle systems
are coherent at corners, which is needed when the
same product is later folded into a compression cup.

\section{Torus excision over the integers}\label{sec:excision}

This section proves the integral mixed excision theorem,
Theorem~\ref{thm:mixed-excision}, and with it
Theorem~\ref{thm:excision-intro}. The torus domains, the closed cut
$Z=D_0\cup(-D_1)\cong(-M)\#M^+\#T^3$ and the reverse excision cobordism
are those of Section~\ref{subsec:handle-geometry}. The gluing tori $T_a\subset D_0=(-U_0)\cup_{T^2}V_0$ and $T_b\subset D_1$ are homologous odd tori in $Z$, the lifting group of $Z$ is generated
by $s_Z=\PD[T_a]\pmod2$, and excising $Z$ along $T_a$ and $T_b$ gives
$Z'=(-M)^\#\sqcup(M^+)^\#$. Write
$\mathsf E:C_G(Z)\to C_G(Z')$ for the excision map of Kronheimer and
Mrowka and $\overline{\mathsf E}$ for the reverse excision map. The
ordinary gauge theory is in Appendix~\ref{app:selfgluing}. There the
almost-complex structures at the closed cut are chosen
with~\eqref{eq:cut-Chern}, so that the conversion at $Z$ is defined on the
quotient complex. Proposition~\ref{prop:integral-excision} then shows that
$\mathsf E$ and $\overline{\mathsf E}$ are inverse over $\ZZ$ up to one
sign, with the lifting groups retained: $\overline{\mathsf E}\mathsf E$ is
chain homotopic to the identity up to one sign, and both maps are chain
homotopy equivalences. Three points of that proof are not in the
literature.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item Regular cut data exist with secondary perturbations supported in
fixed collars of the original ends and invariant under the lifting
groups, while the new three-torus ends carry the unperturbed equation.
\item For the nonseparating three-torus cut of the composite of excision
and reverse excision, the raw determinant-one count is twice the graded
partial trace over the full two-generator complex of the three-torus; the
larger lifting-group quotient is taken afterwards.
\item The parameter-first orientation of homotopies used here and the
fibre-first orientation of \cite[Section~3.9]{KM-unknot} are related by $K=-H_0\eps$, where $K$ and $H_0$ are the homotopies in the two conventions and $\eps$ is the
grading operator.
\end{enumerate}
The mixed content is in Sections~\ref{subsec:torus-lines}
to~\ref{subsec:mixed-excision}: the orientation maps on the separate,
completed and joined torus domains, their compatibility with torus
completion, and the oriented comparison families, which together give the
chain homotopy of Theorem~\ref{thm:mixed-excision}. The position of the
conversion line at the closed cut is fixed by the following convention,
which is also used in Sections~\ref{sec:determinants} and~\ref{sec:cup}.

\begin{convention}\label{conv:interior-line}
The determinant line of the conversion at an interior
cut is placed after the complete input generator orientation line
and before the complete configuration determinant line.
Its position is fixed relative to the entire
configuration, independently of where the geometric
cut is drawn.
\end{convention}

Suppose the configuration is written as a sequence of
glued pieces. Moving an odd conversion line with the
cut through that sequence would move it past the
determinant line of the intervening piece. The sign
would then depend on its index. Convention
\ref{conv:interior-line} avoids this dependence: moving
the line to a fixed reference position costs a sign
determined by the entire configuration, which is
unchanged by moving the cut. At index zero the
alternative fixed placement after the complete
configuration line agrees with this one.

\subsection{The torus domains and their orientation lines}
\label{subsec:torus-lines}
The remainder of this section proves the integral mixed excision
theorem. The torus domains, namely the separate collared domains
$W_{a,\mathsf R}$ ($a=\pm$, $0\le\mathsf R<\infty$), their completions
$W_{a,\infty}$ and the joined domains $W^J_\lambda$ ($\lambda>0$), with the
bridge and the closed cut, are described in
Section~\ref{subsec:handle-geometry}. The unsigned content is Paper~I,
Section~10: the index and energy formulas and the loss formula (Paper~I,
Theorem~10.13 and Proposition~10.14), the torus-completion
bijections~\ref{I3}, the collar and width families, and the closed-cut
decomposition~\ref{I2}. We use those results as they are proved there. The
domain list of Section~\ref{sec:geometry} includes the finite-width
torus domains, their completed limits and the closed-cut mixed
domain, so Proposition~\ref{prop:foundations} and
Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting} apply to them and to
the compact families below. What we add is an orientation for every
count and every one-dimensional family, and the resulting chain
homotopy over $\ZZ$.

At $\mathsf R=0$ the separate domain is the original special
quintuple, and its count is the mixed map $N_a$. For every
$\lambda>0$ the closed cut of~\ref{I2} (Paper~I, Lemma~10.18)
decomposes the
joined domain as
\[
 W^J_\lambda=\overline{\mathcal E}_\lambda\cup_Z M_\lambda .
\]
Here, as in Section~\ref{subsec:handle-geometry},
$\overline{\mathcal E}_\lambda$ is the reverse excision cobordism of
Kronheimer and Mrowka \cite[Section~5.3]{KM-unknot}, with inputs $(-M)^\#$ and $(M^+)^\#$, and
$M_\lambda$ is a mixed domain of DFL type for the pair $(A_0,A_1)$,
with compression bodies $D_{0,\lambda},D_{1,\lambda}$ and single
ordinary input $Z$. The lifting group is the fibre product
$\phi_J=\phi_{\overline{\mathcal E}}\times_{\phi_Z}\phi_M$ over
$\phi_Z=\langle s_Z\rangle$.

\emph{Labels and lines.} An input label is an ordered pair
$a=(a_-,a_+)$ of critical points on $(-M)^\#$ and $(M^+)^\#$, with
generator orientation line $I_a=L_G(a_-)\otimes L_G(a_+)$. An output label is a
point
\[
 x=(x_-,x_+)\in A_0\cap A_1
   =(\widetilde P_0\cap\widetilde P_1)\times(R_0\cap R_1).
\]
Let $C_{S,-}$ and $C_{S,+}$ be the target complexes of $N_-$ and
$N_+$, with their transported orientation systems, and let
$L_\pm(x_\pm)$ be their generator orientation lines. Put
\[
 V_x=L_-(x_-)\otimes L_+(x_+),\qquad
 C_I=C_G((-M)^\#)\otimes^{\mathrm r}C_G((M^+)^\#),\qquad
 C_V=C_{S,-}\otimes^{\mathrm r}C_{S,+}.
\]
In Section~\ref{sec:cup} these are $\CF(\bar p)$ and $\CF(q)$.

Choose the invariant ordinary datum $f_Z$ on $Z$ to vanish on a
neighbourhood of the entire critical set. The determinant-one critical classes on $Z$ over $x$ are exactly
two, as in \cite[Lemmas~2.43 and~2.45]{DFL}: the two genus matching
components permit two central signs, and a constant central gauge on
either compression piece changes both, which leaves $\{1,-1\}^2$
modulo its diagonal. A bundle automorphism of lifting class
$s_Z=\PD[T_a]\pmod2$, the projection of $\cos(\pi\rho)+\sin(\pi\rho)n$
on a genus collar, exchanges them, and it acts freely
(Paper~I, Lemma~10.18(iv), for the lifting classes). Both are irreducible
\cite[Lemma~2.43 and the discussion after (2.44)]{DFL} and, the intersection being
transverse, nondegenerate \cite[Lemma~2.47]{DFL}, so the quotient critical set is
$A_0\cap A_1$. Let $\beta_x$ be either
lift. The complex $C_Z=\CF_{\mathbb X}(A_0,A_1)$ is the target of $N_Z$; its
generator orientation line over $x$ is
\[
 Z_x=L_G^{M}(\beta_x),
\]
the gauge line of $\beta_x$ in the almost-complex structure of the
mixed piece. The two lifts have lines identified by the action of
$s_Z$, as in Proposition~\ref{prop:transport}.

Finally, let $Q_x$ be the conversion line at $\beta_x$ from the
structure induced by $\overline{\mathcal E}_\lambda$ to that of $M_\lambda$, and
let $t$ be its parity. By Section~\ref{sec:gauge}, $t$ is independent
of $x$. Choose one orientation of $Q$ at a reference connection
and transport it along paths of connections on $Z$. Take the structure of $M_\lambda$ with the product germ near the
fibres $T_a,T_b$ on its torus region, as in Section~\ref{subsec:excision-structures},
so that \eqref{eq:cut-Chern} holds for both structures at $Z$. By
Proposition~\ref{prop:character-gauge}, the transport therefore gives
coherent orientations $\vartheta_x$ of all $Q_x$, invariant under $\phi_Z$. The composite $N_Z\overline{\mathsf E}$
always means the composite through the right-placed conversion
$\Theta^{\mathrm r}$ at $Z$, with its line in the position of
Convention~\ref{conv:interior-line}; it has degree $t$.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:CM-invertible}
Let $C_{M,x}$ be the constant pair on $M_\lambda$ over $x$, with limit
$\beta_x$ on its ordinary input, for the primary perturbations alone, with
no secondary or inhomogeneous data. Its linearized mixed operator, with stationary
columns and scalar row, is invertible for every finite $\lambda>0$. It is
also invertible along the path of domains and data used to prove the
deformation invariance~\ref{I1} of the mixed map, which joins $M_{\lambda_0}$ to the special
quintuple of $(D_0,D_1)$ by changing the finite torus-neck lengths and
the compact metric. The inverse bounds are uniform on compact
subfamilies.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The path is that of the deformation invariance~\ref{I1} of the mixed map, as applied
in Paper~I, Proposition~10.21. The lemma is part of
Lemma~\ref{lem:constant-pairs} in
Appendix~\ref{app:inputs}: the kernel vanishes by the quadratic identity
of \cite[Lemma~4.40]{DFL} adapted to these domains, and the index is
zero by \cite[Proposition~3.19]{DFL} at the special quintuple and
continuity along the family. The proof of that lemma uses the local
theory of Section~\ref{app:local}, Lemma~\ref{lem:compact-remainder},
Paper~I, Theorem~10.13, Lemma~\ref{lem:split-preglue}(iii) and
Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing}; none of the inputs of that lemma uses the
present lemma.
\end{proof}

The determinant line of an invertible operator has the canonical
positive element $1$, and conjugation by a gauge transformation
preserves it.

\subsection{Orientation maps on the torus domains}
\label{subsec:torus-maps}
The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:mixed-excision} compares two orientations of
the same index-zero solutions on the joined domains. The closed-cut
orientation $G$ combines the gauge orientation of the reverse excision
cobordism, the conversion at $Z$ and the orientation of the closed-cut
piece $M_\lambda$; along a long closed neck its counts become the composite
$N_M\overline{\mathsf E}$. The product orientation $\Pi$ combines the
orientations of the two separate domains across torus completion, and at
large width its counts are the tensor product
$N_{-,\infty}\otimes^{\mathrm r}N_{+,\infty}$. Proposition~\ref{prop:reference-comparison}
shows that $G$ and $\Pi$ are related by one isomorphism of lines $K_{0,x}$ for
each output label $x$, read off at the joined constant pair.
Proposition~\ref{prop:KR-closed} shows that these isomorphisms form a closed
isomorphism $\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}$ of degree $t$. The one-parameter families of
Sections~\ref{subsec:torus-families} and~\ref{subsec:torus-finite} supply
the remaining homotopies.

All orientation maps below are defined up to positive scalars and take
values in the lines of Section~\ref{subsec:torus-lines}.

\emph{Separate maps.} For a configuration $c$ on $W_{a,\mathsf R}$,
$0\le\mathsf R\le\infty$, Proposition~\ref{prop:transport} gives
\[
 \Gamma^a_c:L_G(a_a)\otimes\det D_c\longrightarrow L_a(x_a).
\]
For a configuration $c$ on the closed-cut piece $M_\lambda$, with
ordinary input $\beta$, the same proposition, with the positive
orientation of Lemma~\ref{lem:CM-invertible} at the terminal constant
pair, gives
\[
 \Gamma^M_c:L^M_G(\beta)\otimes\det D_c\longrightarrow Z_x .
\]

\emph{Strip actions.} For $b\in\{-,+,M\}$ and a regular strip $u$
from $x$ to $y$ at the output of the corresponding domain, glue $u$ to
the constant pair at $x$ and evaluate $\Gamma^b$ on the positive
constant element. This defines an even map
\[
 R_b(u):L_b(x)\otimes\det D_u\longrightarrow L_b(y),
\]
with $L_M(x)=Z_x$. Proposition~\ref{prop:transport} gives the
coherence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:strip-coherence}
 \Gamma^b_{c\#u}\bigl(i\otimes(d\# e)\bigr)
 =R_b(u)\bigl(\Gamma^b_c(i\otimes d)\otimes e\bigr),
\end{equation}
where $d\#e$ is the ordered determinant gluing of $d$ and $e$. The differentials of
$C_{S,\pm}$ and $C_Z$ count index-one strips with these actions. For
a split product strip $u=(u_-,u_+)$ put $n_-=\ind D_{u_-}$ and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:RV}
 R_V(u)(v_-\otimes v_+\otimes e_-\otimes e_+)
 =(-1)^{|v_+|n_-}\,
   R_-(u_-)(v_-\otimes e_-)\otimes R_+(u_+)(v_+\otimes e_+).
\end{equation}
The sign is the move of $\det D_{u_-}$ past $L_+(x_+)$. A regular
index-one product strip for split data has exactly one nonconstant
factor, of index one, since each nonconstant factor has a translation
vector in its kernel and a constant factor at a transverse
intersection is invertible. Thus \eqref{eq:RV} gives the right
tensor differential \eqref{eq:tensor} on $C_V$.

\emph{The decorated closed-cut map.} A configuration on $W^J_\lambda$ is
terminal, in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:mixed-orientation}, when it agrees with a constant
pair $C^c_{M,x}$, for some representative $c$ of the critical point over $x$, on the part of
$M_\lambda$ beyond a parallel copy of $Z$ in the product collar of the cut, and on a two-sided
collar of that copy. Its remaining factor $e$, the
reverse excision cobordism enlarged by the collar, is an arbitrary
configuration with the given inputs. Ordinary gluing at the copy of $Z$
identifies its determinant line with $\det D_e\otimes\det D_{C^c_{M,x}}$,
and the lines at $c$ are identified with those at $\beta_x$ as in
Section~\ref{sec:determinants}. Let
$\gamma_e:I_a\otimes\det D_e\to L^{\overline{\mathcal E}}_G(\beta_x)$ be the
almost-complex orientation of the gauge cobordism, with the ordered
inputs, and let
$\Theta_x:L^{\overline{\mathcal E}}_G(\beta_x)\otimes Q_x\to Z_x$ be the
conversion. Define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:G-terminal}
 G_c(i\otimes \vartheta\otimes d_e\otimes1)
 =(-1)^{t\ind D_e}\,\Theta_x\bigl(\gamma_e(i\otimes d_e)\otimes \vartheta\bigr).
\end{equation}
The sign moves $Q_x$ from its fixed position after the input line to
the position adjacent to the cut. For a general configuration $c$ on
$W^J_\lambda$, the localized several-input shift of
Section~\ref{sec:determinants} (Definition~\ref{def:mixed-orientation}) and
Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting} retains the ordinary cobordism and reaches a
terminal configuration. Transport \eqref{eq:G-terminal}
back along it. This defines an even map
\begin{equation}\label{eq:G-map}
 G_c:I_a\otimes Q_x\otimes\det D_c\longrightarrow Z_x .
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:G-properties}
The map \eqref{eq:G-map} is independent of the shifting path, natural
under $\phi_J$, and continuous on compact families of finite widths.
It has the following compatibilities.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item If $A$ is an ordinary path at the input, then
\[
 G_{A\#c}\bigl(i\otimes \vartheta\otimes(d_A\# d)\bigr)
 =(-1)^{t\ind D_A}G_c\bigl(\gamma_A(i\otimes d_A)\otimes \vartheta\otimes d\bigr).
\]
\item If $u$ is a regular strip at the output, from $x$ to $y$, then,
with the coherent orientations $\vartheta_x,\vartheta_y$,
\[
 G_{c\#u}\bigl(i\otimes \vartheta_y\otimes(d\# e)\bigr)
 =R_M(u)\bigl(G_c(i\otimes \vartheta_x\otimes d)\otimes e\bigr).
\]
\item If $c=A\#_Zc'$ is an ordinary gluing at the closed cut of a
configuration $A$ on $\overline{\mathcal E}_\lambda$ from $a$ to $\beta$ and a
configuration $c'$ on $M_\lambda$ from $\beta$ to $x$, then, with
the coherent orientations $\vartheta=\vartheta_x$ and $\vartheta_\beta$,
\[
 G_c\bigl(i\otimes \vartheta\otimes(d_A\# d')\bigr)
 =(-1)^{t\ind D_A}\,
   \Gamma^M_{c'}\bigl(\Theta_\beta(\gamma_A(i\otimes d_A)\otimes \vartheta_\beta)
                        \otimes d'\bigr).
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Path independence is the square argument in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:transport}, with the relative localized shift of
Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting}(b). Apply it to a path whose endpoints
are terminal. The endpoint tracks and the final edge remain terminal,
where \eqref{eq:G-terminal} is continuous. The orientation double
cover over the parameter square then shows that the original path
preserves \eqref{eq:G-terminal}. Applying this to the concatenation
of two shifting paths from $c$ proves independence. The bundle automorphisms of the
lifting group $\phi_J$ preserve the almost-complex orientations, the lifting-class sign of the conversion on $\phi_Z$ and the positive constant element, and they
map terminal configurations to terminal ones. The pullback of a shifting
path by such an automorphism is a shifting path, so path independence shows
that they preserve \eqref{eq:G-map}.

For (i), the path $A$ is attached at an original input, away from the
localized shift. At a terminal configuration, ordinary associativity
gives $\gamma_{A\#e}(i\otimes(d_A\# d_e))
=\gamma_e(\gamma_A(i\otimes d_A)\otimes d_e)$, and the two sides of
(i) carry the exponents $t(\ind D_A+\ind D_e)$ and
$t\ind D_A+t\ind D_e$. Continuity along the shift gives (i).

For (ii), glue $u$ to the whole shifting family of $c$ with one
long neck. At the terminal end the configuration is $e\#C^c_{M,x}\#u$;
shift $C^c_{M,x}\#u$ to $B\#C^{c'}_{M,y}$ with $B$ an ordinary path on $Z$
of index $n=\ind D_u$, and insert a long product neck at $Z$ so that
$B$ lies on the $\overline{\mathcal E}_\lambda$ side beyond a two-sided
collar of $Z$; then $e\#B\#C^{c'}_{M,y}$ is terminal. The ordinary action of $B$ in the two
structures satisfies the line form of the right conversion
convention \eqref{eq:Theta}:
\[
 \gamma^M_B\bigl(\Theta_x(v\otimes \vartheta_x)\otimes d_B\bigr)
 =(-1)^{tn}\Theta_y\bigl(\gamma^{\overline{\mathcal E}}_B(v\otimes d_B)\otimes \vartheta_y\bigr).
\]
The right side of (ii) at the terminal configuration therefore
carries the exponent $t\ind D_e+tn$. The left side places $Q$ before
the determinant of $e\#B$, with exponent $t(\ind D_e+n)$. The other
factors agree by associativity and the definition of $R_M(u)$.
Continuity gives (ii).

For (iii), shift $c'$ on $M_\lambda$ to $B\#C^{c}_{M,x}$ and glue $A$ to
the whole family at $Z$ with one long neck. Both sides of (iii) are
continuous orientations of the same line along this family. At its
end the configuration $A\#B\#C^{c}_{M,x}$ is terminal, and by
\eqref{eq:G-terminal} its left side carries
$(-1)^{t(\ind D_A+\ind D_B)}$. On the right,
$\Gamma^M_{B\#C^{c}_{M,x}}$ is the action of $B$ in the structure of
$M_\lambda$, which by the display above contributes
$(-1)^{t\ind D_B}$; the prefactor contributes $(-1)^{t\ind D_A}$.
The two sides agree at the end, hence along the family.
\end{proof}

\emph{The reference comparison.} Let $C^{(\lambda)}_{J,x}$ be the
joined constant pair on $W^J_\lambda$ with output $x$. Its incoming
labels are the critical points $a_{x_\pm}$, so its input line is
$L_G(a_{x_-})\otimes L_G(a_{x_+})=V_x$. Its operator is invertible for
every $\lambda$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:split-preglue}(iv) below). Evaluating \eqref{eq:G-map} on
the positive element gives an even isomorphism
$G_{\mathrm{ref}}:V_x\otimes Q_x\to Z_x$. Define the even map
\begin{equation}\label{eq:K0}
 K_{0,x}:Z_x\longrightarrow Q_x\otimes V_x,\qquad
 K_{0,x}G_{\mathrm{ref}}(v\otimes \vartheta)=(-1)^{t|v|}\vartheta\otimes v .
\end{equation}
The lines are fixed and $G_{\mathrm{ref}}$ varies continuously with
$\lambda$, so $K_{0,x}$ does not depend on $\lambda$. Replacing
$C^{(\lambda)}_{J,x}$ by the joined constant pair of another representative
conjugates the operator by a gauge transformation. It is assembled from the
automorphisms relating the representatives on the two sides; these are
central on $T$, and after multiplying one of them by $-1$ they agree there
and glue across the bridge. The positive elements correspond, and so do the
identified lines, by the remark on representatives in
Appendix~\ref{app:shifting}; hence $K_{0,x}$ is unchanged. Let $K^\vartheta_x:Z_x\to V_x$ be
the map defined by $K_{0,x}(z)=\vartheta_x\otimes K^\vartheta_x(z)$, that is, $K_{0,x}$
followed by the isomorphism $Q_x\otimes V_x\to V_x$, $\vartheta_x\otimes v\mapsto v$.
It has degree $t$. Put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:KR-def}
 \mathcal K=K^\vartheta,\qquad \mathcal K^{\mathrm r}=K^\vartheta\eps_{C_Z}^t .
\end{equation}
The isomorphism $\vartheta_x\otimes v\mapsto v$ involves no sign. The map
$\vartheta_x^*\otimes^{\mathrm r}1$ of \eqref{eq:tensor-maps}, with $\vartheta_x^*(\vartheta_x)=1$, differs
from it by $(-1)^{t|v|}$.

\subsection{Product orientations across torus completion}
\label{subsec:torus-split}
On the completed pair $W_{-,\infty}\sqcup W_{+,\infty}$ the product
of the separate maps is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:S-infty}
 \Pi_{(c_-,c_+)}(i_-\otimes i_+\otimes d_-\otimes d_+)
 =(-1)^{|i_+|\ind D_{c_-}}\,
   \Gamma^-_{c_-}(i_-\otimes d_-)\otimes\Gamma^+_{c_+}(i_+\otimes d_+).
\end{equation}
We extend it to large finite widths through the torus cutoff of
Paper~I. On the far torus tail of a completed side write a
configuration as $\alpha+b$ in the torus-fibre slice of Paper~I,
Lemma~10.9. A compact family is \emph{in torus normal form beyond
$r_0$} if every member has this form for $r\ge r_0$, with $b$ bounded
in every weighted norm and with the weighted norms of $b$ on $r\ge r_1$
tending to zero uniformly as $r_1\to\infty$. For a pair $c=(c_-,c_+)$
in torus normal form beyond $r_0$ and $\lambda\ge2r_0+2$, the preglue
$P_\lambda(c)$ replaces $b$ by $\chi_\lambda b$ on both tails, with
$\chi_\lambda(r)=\chi(r-\lambda/2)$, and joins them by $\alpha$ on the
bridge, as in Paper~I, Lemma~10.25. On one side the truncation
$P_{\mathsf R}(c_a)$ on $W_{a,\mathsf R}$ is defined in the same way; it
equals $\alpha$ near the face and so satisfies the flat-fibre
condition.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:split-preglue}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item Every compact family on the completed domains, in the topology of
Section~\ref{sec:geometry}, is in torus normal form beyond some $r_0$
after a continuous family of small even gauge transformations which
are the identity for $r\le r_0-1$ and on the ordinary ends.
\item Let $\mathfrak F$ be a compact family of pairs in torus normal form
beyond $r_0$. The residual $\mathcal F(P_\lambda(c))-\chi_\lambda\mathcal F(c)$,
with $\chi_\lambda$ extended by $1$ off the tails, is supported
where $\chi_\lambda'\neq0$ or $\chi_\lambda^2\neq\chi_\lambda$, and tends to
zero in every weighted norm, uniformly on $\mathfrak F$, as
$\lambda\to\infty$. The same holds for $P_{\mathsf R}$ on one side.
\item The constant pairs $C_{a,x_a}=C^{c_a}$ whose representatives $c_a$
are $\alpha$ on $T$, such as those produced in Appendix~\ref{app:shifting},
are equal to $\alpha$ on the whole added tail. Then
$P_{\mathsf R}(C_{a,x_a})$ is the constant pair
on $W_{a,\mathsf R}$, and
$P_\lambda(C_{-,x_-},C_{+,x_+})=C^{(\lambda)}_{J,x}$ is the joined constant
pair.
\item The operators at these constant pairs are invertible for every
$\mathsf R$ and every $\lambda$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
(i) Continuity and compactness make the set of tail differences
$A-\alpha$ compact in $H^m_\delta$. Their norms on $r\ge r_1$ therefore
tend to zero uniformly as $r_1\to\infty$. Sobolev embedding on unit
boxes, whose geometry is fixed on the flat tail, makes them uniformly
small in $C^1$ beyond some $r_1$. Paper~I, Lemma~10.9, then gives a
unique small even gauge transformation into the torus-fibre slice on
$r\ge r_1$, continuous in the family. Cut off its logarithm on
$[r_1,r_1+1]$, as in Paper~I, Lemma~10.11, and take $r_0=r_1+1$. The
resulting gauges are connected to the identity by the path of
logarithms, so determinant transport along them is canonical, and
every orientation map below is natural under them.

(ii) Only the tail is changed. There the residual is
$(d\chi_\lambda\wedge b)^++(\chi_\lambda^2-\chi_\lambda)(b\wedge b)^+$, which is
bounded by the norms of $b$ on $r\ge\lambda/2-1$.

(iii) Choose the stationary representatives equal to $\alpha$ on the
whole torus region of the slice, not only on the far collar. For a
stationary representative $B=B_T+B_r\,dr$, flat on the torus band
$[b,R]$ and equal to $\alpha$ near $r=R$ (Paper~I, Proposition~10.7),
solve $\partial_rg=-B_rg$, $g(R)=1$, on a slightly larger interval
$[c,R]$ in the determinant-one gauge bundle. Then $B^g$ has no radial
component there, and flatness gives $B^g=\alpha$ on $T\times[b,R]$. With
a cutoff $\eta$ equal to zero near $c$ and one near $[b,\infty)$, the
gauges $q_t(r)=g\bigl(R+t\eta(r)(r-R)\bigr)$, $t\in[0,1]$, form a path
from the identity, supported in a fixed collar, with
$B^{q_1}=\alpha$ on $T\times[b,\infty)$. They preserve the classes of the
perturbed-flat representatives and the genus germs, and depend smoothly on
compact families of primary data. The constant pairs are pullbacks of these representatives, so
they equal $\alpha$ on the tail and the truncation and the preglue have
no cutoff error; the results are the constant pairs on $W_{a,\mathsf R}$
and $W^J_\lambda$, which are solutions.

(iv) This is Lemma~\ref{lem:constant-pairs}.
\end{proof}

The next lemma is the linear gluing across the torus bridge. The
bridge is not an ordinary end, since its cross-section
$T\times\RR_\theta$ runs into the mixed ends, so
Lemma~\ref{lem:line-gluing} does not apply to it.

\begin{lemma}[Determinant lines across torus completion]
\label{lem:bridge-gluing}
Let $\mathfrak F$ be a compact family of pairs in torus normal form
beyond $r_0$. For $c\in\mathfrak F$ let $D_\lambda(c)$ be the linearized
mixed operator at $P_\lambda(c)$, in the realization of
Definition~\ref{def:realization} with the end weights of
Section~\ref{subsec:weights}, and let $D_\infty(c)=D_{c_-}\oplus D_{c_+}$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item There are a finite-dimensional space $E$, a linear map
$\Lambda:E\to Y$ into smooth target sections supported in a compact
subset $K_\Lambda$ of the completed domains, chosen after $\mathfrak F$,
and $\lambda_1$ with $K_\Lambda\subset\{r\le\lambda_1/2\}$, such that
$D_\lambda(c)\oplus\Lambda$ is onto, with a uniformly bounded right
inverse, for all $c\in\mathfrak F$ and $\lambda\in[\lambda_1,\infty]$.
If every $D_\infty(c)$ is onto, one may take $E=0$.
\item The kernels of $D_\lambda(c)\oplus\Lambda$ form a continuous vector
bundle over $\mathfrak F\times[\lambda_1,\infty]$; in particular
$\ind D_\lambda(c)=\ind D_\infty(c)$ for $\lambda\ge\lambda_1$. Near $\lambda=\infty$ its
local frames are $\iota_\lambda(v)=\beta_\lambda v-Q_\lambda(D_\lambda\oplus
\Lambda)\beta_\lambda v$, where $\beta_\lambda$ is multiplication by
$\chi_\lambda$ on both tails and $Q_\lambda$ is the right inverse of (i).
\item Consequently the lines $\det D_\lambda(c)$ form a continuous line
bundle over $\mathfrak F\times[\lambda_1,\infty]$ whose fibre at $\lambda=\infty$
is $\det D_{c_-}\otimes\det D_{c_+}$. This structure does not depend on
$\Lambda$. Where the operators are invertible, the canonical positive
elements form a continuous section.
\item Let an ordinary path or strip be glued, with a long neck, at an
original gauge input or at the strip output, to every member of
$\mathfrak F$. The identification of (iii) commutes with the ordinary
gluing isomorphism of Lemma~\ref{lem:line-gluing}.
\end{enumerate}
The same statements hold for a compact family in torus normal form on
one completed side and its truncations $P_{\mathsf R}(c)$ on
$W_{a,\mathsf R}$, $\mathsf R\to\infty$. They also hold for every family of
operators with the properties listed at the beginning of its proof in
Appendix~\ref{app:bridge-gluing}, in
particular for the linearizations in the torus-completion chart (Paper~I,
Definition~10.23) and for the interpolating operators in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-path}.
\end{lemma}
The proof is given in Appendix~\ref{app:bridge-gluing}. Once a uniformly
bounded right inverse is available, the construction is the standard
gluing of determinant lines along a long neck, as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:line-gluing}. Two points are specific to the torus bridge.
First, the uniform estimates must be proved directly, because the
cross-section of the bridge runs into the mixed ends; they are obtained by
patching the flat odd-torus model on the bridge with the stationary models
on the mixed ends, as for the uniform inverse of Paper~I,
Proposition~10.27. Second, the stationary columns at finite and at infinite
width differ on the whole mixed ends, not only on the tails; their
difference tends to zero exponentially in the width, because of the fibre
gap $\mu_T$ on the torus neck.

Let $\mathfrak F$ be a compact family of pairs of configurations on the
completed domains. By Lemma~\ref{lem:split-preglue}(i) we may assume it
is in torus normal form. Examples are a pair of index-zero solutions
and a pair of constant pairs with strips glued at their outputs. For
$c\in\mathfrak F$ define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:S-def}
 \Pi_{P_\lambda(c)}:I_a\otimes\det D_{P_\lambda(c)}\longrightarrow V_x ,
 \qquad\lambda\ge\lambda_1,
\end{equation}
as the continuous extension of \eqref{eq:S-infty} along
$\{P_\lambda(c)\}_{\lambda\in[\lambda_1,\infty]}$ given by
Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing}. It is continuous on
$\mathfrak F\times[\lambda_1,\infty]$ and natural under the joined lifting
group $\phi_J\cong\phi_-\oplus\phi_+$.

The separate shifts of Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting} give, for
$c\in\mathfrak F$, a homotopy $\nu\mapsto H_\nu(c)$, $\nu\in[0,1]$, continuous in
the topology of Section~\ref{sec:geometry}, from $c$ to
$(A_-\#C^{c_-}_{-,x_-},A_+\#C^{c_+}_{+,x_+})$ with $A_\pm$ ordinary paths
and representatives $c_\pm$ which are $\alpha$ on $T$. Its image
is compact. By Lemma~\ref{lem:split-preglue}(i) it can be put in torus
normal form beyond a common $r_0$, and by
Lemma~\ref{lem:split-preglue}(iii) its endpoints equal $\alpha$ on the
tails. Applying \eqref{eq:S-def} to this larger compact family gives
$\Pi$ on the rectangle $\{P_\lambda(H_\nu(c))\}$. At $\lambda=\infty$ it is
\eqref{eq:S-infty}, which along $\nu$ is the transport defining
$\Gamma^\pm$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:transport}. At $\nu=1$ the
configuration is $(A_-\sqcup A_+)\#C^{(\lambda)}_{J,x}$, and there $\Pi$ is
the ordinary orientation of $A_-\sqcup A_+$ followed by the positive
element of the invertible constant factor, by
Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing}(iii),(iv). The rectangle is
contractible, so this description of $\Pi$ at $P_\lambda(c)$ through $\nu=1$
does not depend on the chosen shifting homotopy.

The same device compares the separate orientations on the finite and
completed domains. By construction, $\Gamma^a$ on the finite domain
$W_{a,\mathsf R}$ is a continuous orientation over its configuration
space, defined by the one-input shift on that domain.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:separate-completion}
Let $\mathfrak F$ be a compact family on $W_{a,\infty}$ in torus normal
form. For $\mathsf R\ge\mathsf R_1$, the orientation $\Gamma^a$ of
$W_{a,\mathsf R}$ at $P_{\mathsf R}(c)$ is the continuous extension of
$\Gamma^a_c$ along $\{P_{\mathsf R}(c)\}_{\mathsf R\in[\mathsf R_1,\infty]}$
given by Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Consider the rectangle $\{P_{\mathsf R}(H_\nu(c))\}$, with $H_\nu$ the
separate shift in torus normal form. The extension from
$\mathsf R=\infty$ is continuous on it by Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing}.
For fixed finite $\mathsf R$ the orientation $\Gamma^a$ of $W_{a,\mathsf R}$
is continuous in $\nu$. At $\nu=1$ the configuration is
$A\#P_{\mathsf R}(C_{a,x})$, and $P_{\mathsf R}(C_{a,x})$ is the constant
pair of $W_{a,\mathsf R}$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:split-preglue}(iii). There
$\Gamma^a$ is the ordinary orientation of $A$ followed by the positive
element of the constant factor. The extension from $\mathsf R=\infty$ is
the same: by Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing}(iv) it commutes with gluing
$A$, and the constant factor is invertible for every $\mathsf R$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:constant-pairs}) with
positive element continuous up to $\mathsf R=\infty$.
The two orientations therefore agree at $\nu=1$, and hence on the whole
rectangle.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:strip-actions}
The strip actions $R_\pm(u)$ computed on $W_{\pm,\mathsf R}$ do not depend
on $\mathsf R\in[0,\infty]$, and $R_M(u)$ computed on $M_\lambda$ does not
depend on $\lambda$ and agrees with the action on the special quintuple
of $(D_0,D_1)$ defining $N_Z$. Thus each of $C_{S,\pm}$ and $C_Z$ carries
one orientation system.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Each action is an isomorphism between fixed lines, defined through an
invertible constant pair with a strip glued at its output. For finite
$\mathsf R$ it varies continuously with $\mathsf R$, since the constant
pairs are invertible for every $\mathsf R$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:constant-pairs}); at $\mathsf R=\infty$ use
Lemma~\ref{lem:separate-completion} with $\mathfrak F=\{C_{a,x}\#u\}$,
which is $\alpha$ on the tail, together with
Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing}(iv) for the strip glued at the output. For $R_M$ use the width interval and the
family of Paper~I, Lemma~9.7, along which $C_{M,x}$ stays invertible
by Lemma~\ref{lem:CM-invertible}.
A continuous family of isomorphisms between fixed lines is constant.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:reference-comparison}
On the family $P_\lambda(H_\nu(c))$, $c\in\mathfrak F$, for $\lambda\ge\lambda_1$
and $\nu\in[0,1]$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:reference-comparison}
 K_{0,x}G_c=(1_{Q_x}\otimes \Pi_c)(\sigma_{I,Q}\otimes1_{\det}),
\end{equation}
where $\sigma_{I,Q}$ moves $Q_x$ past the entire input line. Let
$G^{\vartheta}_c(i\otimes d)=G_c(i\otimes \vartheta_x\otimes d)$ be the map obtained
by evaluating $G_c$ on $\vartheta_x$ in its middle factor, and let
$m=\ind D_c\pmod2$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:KR-G}
 \mathcal K\,G^{\vartheta}_c=(-1)^{t|I|}\Pi_c,\qquad
 \mathcal K^{\mathrm r}\,G^{\vartheta}_c=(-1)^{t(m+1)}\Pi_c .
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Both sides of \eqref{eq:reference-comparison} are continuous on the
rectangle at finite $\lambda$, so it suffices to verify it at $\nu=1$.
There the configuration is $(A_-\sqcup A_+)\#C^{(\lambda)}_{J,x}$.
Let $k_A$ be the total parity of $\det D_{A_-}\otimes\det D_{A_+}$ and
let $v=\gamma_A(i\otimes d_A)\in V_x$, so $|v|=|i|+k_A$. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:G-properties}(i),
$G_c(i\otimes \vartheta\otimes(d_A\#1))
=(-1)^{tk_A}G_{\mathrm{ref}}(v\otimes \vartheta)$, and by \eqref{eq:K0}
\[
 K_{0,x}G_c(i\otimes \vartheta\otimes(d_A\#1))
 =(-1)^{tk_A+t|v|}\vartheta\otimes v=(-1)^{t|i|}\vartheta\otimes \Pi_c(i\otimes d).
\]
This is the right side of \eqref{eq:reference-comparison}. For the
second assertion, the tensor of maps $1_Q\otimes \Pi_c$ with even $\Pi_c$
introduces no sign, so removing the factor $\vartheta_x$ as in the definition of
$K^\vartheta_x$ gives $\mathcal KG^{\vartheta}_c=(-1)^{t|I|}\Pi_c$. Since $G_c$ is
even, $|G^{\vartheta}_c(i\otimes d)|=|i|+t+m$, and $\eps^t$ contributes
$(-1)^{t(|i|+t+m)}$. The product is $(-1)^{t(t+m)}=(-1)^{t(m+1)}$.
\end{proof}

At index zero the coefficient in \eqref{eq:KR-G} is $(-1)^t$; at the
isolated points of a one-parameter family, where $m$ is odd, it is
$+1$.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:KR-closed}
$\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}:C_Z\to C_V$ is an isomorphism of graded groups, and
\[
 d_V\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}=(-1)^t\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}d_Z .
\]
Thus $\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}$ is a closed isomorphism of degree $t$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
$K_{0,x}$ is an isomorphism of lines, and the generators of $C_Z$ and
$C_V$ are both indexed by $A_0\cap A_1$; hence $\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}$ is an
isomorphism. Let $u=(u_-,u_+)$ be a regular index-one product strip
from $x$ to $y$ for the split data. Apply
Proposition~\ref{prop:reference-comparison} to the pair of separate
configurations $(C_{-,x_-}\#u_-,C_{+,x_+}\#u_+)$, which is in torus
normal form, and to its preglue $C^{(\lambda)}_{J,x}\#u$. By
\eqref{eq:strip-coherence} on each side and \eqref{eq:S-infty}, the
product orientation at $\lambda=\infty$ is
\[
 \Pi_{C_J\#u}\bigl(v\otimes(1\# e)\bigr)=R_V(u)(v\otimes e).
\]
Indeed, $\Gamma^\pm_{C_\pm}(v_\pm\otimes1)=v_\pm$, the determinant of
$C_{\pm}\#u_\pm$ is $(1\# e_\pm)$ with
$\ind D_{C_-\#u_-}=n_-$, and \eqref{eq:strip-coherence} gives
$\Gamma^\pm(v_\pm\otimes(1\# e_\pm))=R_\pm(u_\pm)(v_\pm\otimes e_\pm)$.
Formula \eqref{eq:S-infty} with $i_\pm=v_\pm$ then contributes
$(-1)^{|v_+|n_-}$, which is the sign in \eqref{eq:RV}; reordering the
even constant elements costs nothing. By continuity in $\lambda$, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing}(iv) for the strip glued at the output,
the identity holds at large finite widths. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:G-properties}(ii) with $c=C^{(\lambda)}_{J,x}$,
\[
 G_{C_J\#u}\bigl(v\otimes \vartheta_y\otimes(1\# e)\bigr)
 =R_M(u)\bigl(G_{\mathrm{ref}}(v\otimes \vartheta_x)\otimes e\bigr).
\]
Apply $K_{0,y}$ and use \eqref{eq:reference-comparison} for
$C_J\#u$ and \eqref{eq:K0}. Both sides of the resulting identity,
evaluated on $G_{\mathrm{ref}}(v\otimes \vartheta_x)\otimes e$, equal
$(-1)^{t|v|}\vartheta_y\otimes R_V(u)(v\otimes e)$. Let $J_u:Q_x\to Q_y$ be the conversion transport along the ordinary
path obtained by shifting $C_{M,x}\#u$; by coherence $J_u\vartheta_x=\vartheta_y$.
Hence
\[
 K_{0,y}R_M(u)=(J_u\otimes R_V(u))(K_{0,x}\otimes1_{\det D_u}),
\]
with $Q$ to the left of both $V$ and the strip determinant; since
$R_V(u)$ is even, the tensor of maps inserts no sign. Removing the
factors $\vartheta_x$ and $\vartheta_y$ as in the definition of $K^\vartheta$ gives
$K^\vartheta_yR_M(u)(z\otimes e)=R_V(u)(K^\vartheta_xz\otimes e)$. Summing over
index-one strips gives $d_V\mathcal K=\mathcal Kd_Z$. Since
$d_Z\eps^t=(-1)^t\eps^td_Z$, the assertion for $\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}$ follows.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Signed counts at large torus length}
\label{subsec:torus-counts}
Fix regular data in the following order, as in Paper~I, Section~10.11:
the separate mixed maps $N_\pm$, the closed-cut and joined data at a width
$\lambda_0\ge\lambda_*$, regular data on the completed domains, and lengths
$\mathsf R\ge\mathsf R_0$ and $\lambda\ge\lambda_1$. The completed
index-zero sets are finite and regular, and there are no completed
solutions of negative index. By the torus completion~\ref{I3} (Paper~I, Theorem~10.32(i) and~(ii)), the gluing
maps $\mathcal G_{\mathsf R}$ and $\mathcal G_\lambda$ are bijections onto
the regular index-zero solutions on $W_{a,\mathsf R}$ and $W^J_\lambda$, and
there are no solutions of negative index on these domains. Write
$N_{a,\infty}$ for the completed separate counts, oriented by
$\Gamma^a$, and $N_a^{(\mathsf R)}$ for the counts on $W_{a,\mathsf R}$.
On $W^J_\lambda$ let $F^\Pi_\lambda:C_I\to C_V$ count index-zero
solutions oriented by $\Pi$, and $F^Z_\lambda:C_I\to C_Z$ the same
solutions oriented by $G^{\vartheta}$; their degrees are $0$ and $t$.
The orientation $\Pi$ at these solutions is defined by the following
lemma.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:gluing-path}
Let $V$ be a completed index-zero solution, or a pair of them. For
$\mathsf L\ge\mathsf L_1$ let
$\mathcal G_{\mathsf L}(V)=\Psi_{\mathsf L}(z_{\mathsf L}(V))$ be the glued
solution of~\ref{I3}, where $\Psi_{\mathsf L}$ is the gluing chart at the
preglued configuration $P_{\mathsf L}(V)$ and $z_{\mathsf L}(V)$ is the
unique small zero in that chart (Paper~I, Proposition~10.30). Consider
the rectangle of configurations
\[
 \mathcal R_V=\{P_{\mathsf L}(V):\mathsf L\in[\mathsf L_1,\infty]\}
 \cup\{\Psi_{\mathsf L}(sz_{\mathsf L}(V)):s\in[0,1],\
                  \mathsf L\in[\mathsf L_1,\infty)\},
\]
with $P_\infty(V)=V$. Here $V$ is represented in the gauge in which its
torus tail lies in the torus-fibre slice (the adapted representative of
Paper~I, Lemmas~10.11 and~10.25), so that the two edges meet, and the
gauge transformation of Lemma~\ref{lem:split-preglue}(i) is the identity by
the uniqueness of that slice (Paper~I, Lemma~10.9). The determinant lines
of the linearized mixed operators form a continuous line bundle over
$\mathcal R_V$, and determinant transport carries the canonical positive
element at $V$ to the canonical positive element at
$\mathcal G_{\mathsf L}(V)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Over the first edge the line bundle is that of
Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing} with $\mathfrak F=\{V\}$, and its
extension clause covers the chart operators used below; the second edge is a
continuous family on a fixed domain for each $\mathsf L$.

Use also the chart operators $T$ of Paper~I, Definition~10.23. At the
preglues they are invertible with a uniform bound by Paper~I,
Proposition~10.27; at $\mathsf L=\infty$ the chart is centred at $V$
and the operator is invertible, since it has index zero and is injective
at the regular completed solution $V$, by the argument of Paper~I,
Proposition~10.27.
Along the contraction path the chart operator is
$D_{\mathsf L}+DN_{\mathsf L}(sz_{\mathsf L})$ with
$\|DN_{\mathsf L}(sz_{\mathsf L})\|\le2C_{\rm quad}r\le(2C_{\rm inv})^{-1}$,
by the quadratic estimate of Paper~I, Lemma~10.29(c), and the choice of
radius in Paper~I, Proposition~10.30, so it is invertible.
The chart operator at $\Psi_{\mathsf L}(sz)$ is centred at the preglue.
Moving its slice centre, trace chart and target projection to the
configuration itself changes it by at most $C\|sz\|$ in operator norm,
which is small, so the straight path between them consists of invertible
operators. Here both operators are read in the fixed end trivializations,
and $\|D\Psi_{\mathsf L}(sz)-D\Psi_{\mathsf L}(0)\|\le C\|sz\|$ by the
second-derivative bounds of the face chart (Paper~I, Lemma~10.28(c)) and
of the genus chart (Paper~I, Lemma~10.29(c)).
The resulting operator and the realization of
Definition~\ref{def:realization} at the same
configuration have the same decaying block; their stationary columns
and scalar rows differ by a bounded finite-rank map (Paper~I, Remark~10.24). The straight path between these two is
therefore a continuous Fredholm path, and these identifications are
continuous over $\mathcal R_V$. The positive elements of the invertible
chart operators thus give a continuous nonvanishing section of the
determinant bundle of the linearized mixed operators.

At the two solutions $V$ and $\mathcal G_{\mathsf L}(V)$ this section is
the canonical positive element. Indeed, there the change of stationary
columns is unipotent: the chart lifts and the Coulomb lifts differ by
infinitesimal gauge directions $d\psi_e$ and by decaying fields already
in the domain; this holds because both lift the same deformation of the stationary
limit with zero normal component, and the corrected lifts differ from the
uncorrected ones by decaying fields (Paper~I, (10.5)). A unipotent change of
source coordinates preserves the positive element.
The change of the scalar row is block triangular with invertible gauge
block, as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:realization}. At $V$ the gauge
block is the Neumann map of Paper~I, Lemma~10.26(ii), with
$\mathsf L=\infty$. At $\mathcal G_{\mathsf L}(V)$ the $C^m$ hypothesis of
that lemma is not available, since Paper~I, Proposition~10.30, bounds
$z_{\mathsf L}(V)$ only in $X_{\mathsf L}$; there the gauge block is
invertible by Proposition~\ref{prop:foundations}(b), the connection being
irreducible on the odd surface and torus collars. At these points every
operator of the interpolating paths
is invertible.
\end{proof}

Extend \eqref{eq:S-def} from $P_\lambda(V)$ to $\mathcal G_\lambda(V)$ along
the second edge of $\mathcal R_V$. Both sides of
\eqref{eq:reference-comparison} are continuous along it, so the
comparison persists at $\mathcal G_\lambda(V)$.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:large-length-counts}
Fix the data as at the beginning of this subsection, and let
$N^{(\mathsf R)}_a$, $N_{a,\infty}$, $F^\Pi_\lambda$ and $F^Z_\lambda$ be the
counts defined there, oriented by $\Gamma^a$, $\Pi$ and $G^{\vartheta}$. Then,
for $\mathsf R$ and $\lambda$ sufficiently large,
\[
 N^{(\mathsf R)}_a=N_{a,\infty}\ (a=\pm),\qquad
 F^\Pi_\lambda=N_{-,\infty}\otimes^{\mathrm r}N_{+,\infty},\qquad
 \mathcal K^{\mathrm r}F^Z_\lambda=(-1)^t\,N_{-,\infty}\otimes^{\mathrm r}N_{+,\infty}.
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Let $V$ be a completed index-zero solution on a separate side. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:separate-completion} with $\mathfrak F=\{V\}$, the
orientation $\Gamma^a$ of $W_{a,\mathsf R}$ at $P_{\mathsf R}(V)$ is the
continuous extension of $\Gamma^a_V$; along the second edge of
$\mathcal R_V$ it is continuous on the fixed domain. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-path} the canonical positive element is also
transported continuously from $V$ to $\mathcal G_{\mathsf R}(V)$. Hence $\Gamma^a(i\otimes1)$ has the
same sign at $\mathcal G_{\mathsf R}(V)$ as at $V$. The bijection
$\mathcal G_{\mathsf R}$ preserves labels and lifting orbits, so the
counts agree entry by entry.

In the joined case $\Pi$ at $\mathcal G_\lambda(V)$ is, by definition, the
continuous extension of \eqref{eq:S-infty} over $\mathcal R_V$, and the
same lemma transports the positive element. At index zero
$\ind D_{V_-}=0$, so \eqref{eq:S-infty} evaluated on the positive
element is the product of the two separate signs. The joined lifting
group is $\phi_-\oplus\phi_+$, and $\mathcal G_\lambda$ is a bijection
from pairs of classes with no further gluing parameter~\ref{I3}, so the
orbit counts multiply. The generator
factors in \eqref{eq:S-infty} are those of the right tensor of maps
\eqref{eq:tensor-maps} of the even maps $N_{\pm,\infty}$. This proves
the second identity. The third follows from the second and
\eqref{eq:KR-G} with $m=0$, applied solution by solution, since
\eqref{eq:reference-comparison} holds at $\mathcal G_\lambda(V)$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Boundary orientation of the comparison families}
\label{subsec:torus-families}
The comparison families below are those of Paper~I:
Propositions~10.16 and~10.17 for collar length and width, with the
weighted parameter column of~\ref{I9}, Lemma~10.20 for the change of data
at fixed width, the relative regularization of~\ref{I7} (Paper~I,
Theorem~5.4) for compactly supported changes of secondary data, and the
mixed-end family of the deformation invariance~\ref{I1} of the mixed map, as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:vertex-id}(V2), which produces $H_M$ in
Proposition~\ref{prop:closed-neck}. In each case the data are constant
near $b=0,1$ and the endpoints are regular in index at most one. A
relative invariant regularization then gives finitely many isolated
parametrized solutions of vertical index $-1$, each with invertible
augmented operator $(v,\tau)\mapsto Dv+\tau C$. It also gives a compact
one-manifold of parametrized index-zero solutions, whose boundary
consists of the two endpoint fibres and one branch for each pair of an
index $-1$ solution with an index-one trajectory at an ordinary end.
Here $C$ is the parameter column. On a varying mixed end it is the
weighted column of~\ref{I9}: since the stationary references solve the
equation for every parameter, the parameter derivative lies in the
weighted target, although it is not compactly supported.

Let $\Omega$ denote the orientation map in use: $\Gamma^a$ on a
separate domain, $\Gamma^M$ on the closed-cut piece, or $G$ on a joined
domain. Put $f=0$ in the first two cases and $f=t$ in the third. The
endpoint counts $F_0,F_1$ are oriented by $\Omega$ on the positive
element. At an isolated parametrized solution $c$, orient the parameter
first: choose $d_c\in\det D_c$ such that $db\otimes d_c$ is the positive
element of the invertible augmented operator, and count $c$ by
$\Omega(i\otimes d_c)$, or $\Omega(i\otimes \vartheta\otimes d_c)$ when $f=t$. Let
$H$ be the resulting count. Its degree is $f+1$.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:family-signs}
Let one of the comparison families above be regularized relatively as
described, and let $\Omega$, $f$, the endpoint counts $F_0,F_1$ and the
count $H$ of its isolated parametrized solutions of vertical index $-1$ be
as above. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:family-homotopy}
 F_1-F_0=d_{\rm out}H+(-1)^fHd_{\rm in}=\delta H .
\end{equation}
For a fixed regular datum, the index-one boundary gives
$d_{\rm out}F=(-1)^fFd_{\rm in}$, so $F$ is closed of degree $f$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Orient the parametrized index-zero space by $\det(T_b[0,1])\otimes\det D$
and $\Omega$, as in Section~\ref{sec:determinants}; the boundary is
oriented by the outward normal first. At $b=1$ the outward normal is
$\partial_b$, and at $b=0$ it is $-\partial_b$; there the vertical
operators are invertible, so these contribute $F_1-F_0$.

At an outgoing strip break $(c,u)$ let $p_T$ be the preglued
configuration of the augmented pair, with the strip translated to
$u(s-T,\cdot)$. In the gluing of~\ref{I7} (Paper~I, Theorem~5.1(iv)), for an index
$-1$ piece of a family, the glued solutions are $U_T=P_{p_T}(z_T)$.
Here $P_{p_T}$ is the chart of Paper~I, Lemma~5.3, and
$z_T\in\im Q_{p_T}$, a parameter increment together with a field, is the
fixed point
\[
 z_T=-Q'_{p_T}\bigl(\widehat{\mathcal G}_{p_T}(0)+N_{p_T}(z_T)\bigr).
\]
The operator $Q_{p_T}$ is the patched right inverse of the augmented operator at
$p_T$, whose image is complementary to the cut-off translation vector,
$Q'_{p_T}$ is the right inverse of $D\widehat{\mathcal G}_{p_T}(0)$ with
the same image, and the map
$z\mapsto-Q'_{p_T}(\widehat{\mathcal G}_{p_T}(0)+N_{p_T}(z))$ is a
contraction with constant at most $\frac12$. Identify the domains by maps
which fix the mixed core and translate the strip core, and patch
$Q_{p_T}$ with cutoffs translated together with the strip. Then
$\widehat{\mathcal G}_{p_T}$, $N_{p_T}$ and $Q'_{p_T}$ are differentiable
in $T$ with bounded derivatives, the $T$-derivative of
$\widehat{\mathcal G}_{p_T}(0)$ is exponentially small in $T$, and the
$T$-derivative of $p_T$ is the cut-off translation vector $-\partial_su$
plus an error which is exponentially small in $T$. Differentiate the
fixed-point identity in $T$ and absorb the term containing the
derivative of $z_T$ by the contraction constant. This bounds the
$T$-derivative of $z_T$ by a constant times
$\|\widehat{\mathcal G}_{p_T}(0)\|+\|z_T\|$ plus the $T$-derivative of
$\widehat{\mathcal G}_{p_T}(0)$, and all three tend to zero. For
pseudoholomorphic curves this $T$-derivative estimate, with exponential
decay, is \cite[Theorem~6.4]{FOOO-decay}; the same argument applies with
the fixed mixed core and on the fixed ordinary gauge end.
Hence $\partial_TU_T=-\partial_su+o(1)$ on the strip core after
projection to the slice. Linear gluing (Lemma~\ref{lem:line-gluing})
identifies $\det A_c\otimes\det D_u$ with the determinant along the
branch, and the positive translation vector $\partial_su$ orients
$\det D_u$. The outward direction at $T=\infty$ is $\partial_T$, which
is negative in this order. With the parameter already first in
$\det A_c=\det(T_b[0,1])\otimes\det D_c$ and
\eqref{eq:strip-coherence} or Lemma~\ref{lem:G-properties}(ii), this
branch contributes $-d_{\rm out}H$.

At an incoming break $(A,c)$, with $A$ an index-one trajectory, the
trajectory is translated to $A(s+T,\cdot)$, and the same argument gives
$\partial_TU_T=+\partial_sA+o(1)$, positive in
$\det D_A\otimes\det A_c$. The outward direction is again $\partial_T$.
Moving the one-dimensional parameter line to the front past the odd
line $\det D_A$ contributes $-1$. The input compatibility of $\Omega$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:transport} for $\Gamma$,
Lemma~\ref{lem:G-properties}(i) for $G$) contributes $(-1)^{f}$,
because $\ind D_A=1$. This branch contributes $-(-1)^fHd_{\rm in}$.
For a joined domain with two inputs, the other-arm permutations are
those of the right tensor differential, by
Lemma~\ref{lem:tensor-geometric}. The total signed boundary vanishes,
which is \eqref{eq:family-homotopy}. Every branch is counted once with
its matched lifting orbit. The relative regularization of~\ref{I7} gives
one branch for each pair (Paper~I, Theorem~5.4(ii)). The gluing of~\ref{I7}
matches lifting orbits, since the lifting group of the glued problem is
the fibre product of those of the pieces (Paper~I, Theorem~5.1(ii),(iv),
under hypothesis (H5) there).

For a fixed datum the same computation without the parameter line
gives $-d_{\rm out}F+(-1)^fFd_{\rm in}=0$. The sign determinations use
only the direction of the translation vector, the ordinary linear
gluing of Lemma~\ref{lem:line-gluing} at the ordinary end where the
break occurs, and the invertibility of the bordered augmented operator
along the gluing paths. By Corollary~\ref{cor:strip-actions} the
output differential is the same at every point of the family.
\end{proof}

For a simple check of the incoming sign, take $D_A:\RR v\to0$,
$D_c:0\to\RR y$ and $C:\RR\mu\to\RR y$ the identity. In the order
$\det D_A\otimes\det A_c$ the kernel is oriented by $v$. In the
parameter-first order $\mu\otimes v\otimes y^*$ cancelling $\mu$
against $y$ leaves $-v$.

\subsection{Chain homotopies across collar length, width and the closed neck}
\label{subsec:torus-finite}
\begin{proposition}\label{prop:finite-families}
Let $N_\pm$ be the regular mixed maps at $\mathsf R=0$, and fix a
regular joined datum at a width $\lambda_0>0$. Choose $\mathsf R$ and a width
$\lambda_2$ as in Proposition~\ref{prop:large-length-counts}, and at these
lengths add small invariant secondary perturbations supported in the
cores which make index one regular and preserve the index-zero
counts. Then there are maps $P_\pm$ of degree one and $B_J$ of degree
$t+1$ with
\[
 N_{a,\infty}-N_a=\delta P_a,\qquad
 F^Z_{\lambda_2}-F^Z_{\lambda_0}=\delta B_J .
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The added perturbations exist as in Paper~I, Section~10.11: the
index-zero sets are finite and regular, so openness and compactness
preserve them, their signs and the absence of negative indices. For the
separate side, join the data at $\mathsf R=0$ to those at the large
length by one family which first changes the collar length and then
makes a compactly supported change of secondary data. Its endpoints are
regular through index one. Paper~I, Proposition~10.16, with the weighted
parameter column of~\ref{I9}, and the relative regularization of~\ref{I7}
give its relative regularization and compactification, applied to the
concatenated family, whose two segments each satisfy the hypotheses
of~\ref{I9} (Paper~I, Lemma~10.15), and Lemma~\ref{lem:family-signs} with
$\Omega=\Gamma^a$ gives $N^{(\mathsf R)}_a-N_a=\delta P_a$. Proposition~\ref{prop:large-length-counts}
replaces $N^{(\mathsf R)}_a$ by $N_{a,\infty}$. For the joined side use
one family consisting of the width change of Paper~I,
Proposition~10.17, from $\lambda_0$ to $\lambda_2$, followed by the change
of secondary data at width $\lambda_2$, regularize it relatively as a
single family, and apply Lemma~\ref{lem:family-signs} with
$\Omega=G$. The joined families lie
at finite widths throughout, so $G$ is defined along them.
\end{proof}

With the right tensor convention,
$\delta(F\otimes^{\mathrm r}G)=(-1)^{|G|}\delta F\otimes^{\mathrm r}G+F\otimes^{\mathrm r}\delta G$.
Since $N_\pm$ and $N_{\pm,\infty}$ are even and closed,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:P-homotopy}
 P=P_-\otimes^{\mathrm r}N_++N_{-,\infty}\otimes^{\mathrm r}P_+,\qquad
 \delta P=N_{-,\infty}\otimes^{\mathrm r}N_{+,\infty}-N_-\otimes^{\mathrm r}N_+ .
\end{equation}
By Proposition~\ref{prop:KR-closed} and \eqref{eq:compose-homotopy},
$\delta(\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}B_J)=(-1)^t\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}\delta B_J$. Together with
Proposition~\ref{prop:large-length-counts} this gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:width-homotopy}
 (-1)^t\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}F^Z_{\lambda_0}-N_-\otimes^{\mathrm r}N_+
 =\delta\bigl(P-\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}B_J\bigr).
\end{equation}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:closed-neck}
Let $N_M$ be the count on the closed-cut piece $M_{\lambda_0}$ of the
decomposition~\ref{I2}, oriented by $\Gamma^M$. Its data are the ordinary
datum $f_Z$ and the metrics on $Z$ used for $N_Z$ at width $\lambda_0$, and
the regular invariant secondary data chosen on this piece in the closed-cut
data (Paper~I, Section~10.9). There are
maps $B_{\rm cut}$ of degree $t+1$ and $H_M$ of degree one with
\[
 N_M\overline{\mathsf E}-F^Z_{\lambda_0}=\delta B_{\rm cut},\qquad
 N_Z-N_M=\delta H_M .
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Paper~I, Proposition~10.21, inserts a closed neck $[-\ell,\ell]\times Z$
carrying the cut-off term $f_Z$. It shows that for large $\ell$, after
a small invariant perturbation making index one regular, the joined
index-zero solutions are exactly the gluings $A\#_Zc'$ of index-zero
solutions on $\overline{\mathcal E}$ and on $M_{\lambda_0}$, one for each
matched lifting orbit. The lifting group is the fibre product over $\phi_Z$
and all three pieces are connected, so by
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-multiplicity} the gluing fibre is trivial and the
quotient count is the matrix composite. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:G-properties}(iii), at index zero the orientation $G$ of
$A\#_Zc'$ is $\Gamma^M_{c'}$ applied to the right-placed conversion of
$\gamma_A$; the prefactor $(-1)^{t\ind D_A}$ is one. Linear gluing along
the long neck is continuous and the operators along the gluing paths
are invertible, so the sign is that of the composite. Hence the count
at the long-neck data is $N_M\overline{\mathsf E}$. Paper~I,
Lemma~10.20, connects the data at width $\lambda_0$ to the long-neck
data by a family with compactly supported parameter derivative, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:family-signs} with $\Omega=G$ gives $B_{\rm cut}$.

For the second identity, the path of domains and data of the deformation invariance~\ref{I1}
of the mixed map, as applied in Paper~I, Proposition~10.21, changes
the finite
torus-neck lengths of
$D_{i,\lambda_0}$ to those of the special quintuple of $(D_0,D_1)$,
together with a compact interpolation of the metric, with the ordinary
datum on $Z$ fixed. This is a noncollapsing family with the weighted
parameter column of~\ref{I9}. Concatenate it with the
compactly supported change from the secondary data of $M_{\lambda_0}$ to
those of $N_Z$ (relative regularization~\ref{I7}), and regularize the concatenated
family relatively as a single family; this is the family used in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:vertex-id}(V2), which applies verbatim to $M_{\lambda_0}$, a domain of DFL type in the class of~\ref{I1} with the ordinary end data of $N_Z$, whose constant pairs are invertible along it by Lemma~\ref{lem:constant-pairs}(c); it is traversed in the opposite direction, so that $H_M=-H_{M_{\lambda_0}}$.
Lemma~\ref{lem:family-signs} with $\Omega=\Gamma^M$ gives $H_M$. The input complex $C_G(Z)$ is the
same at both ends, since $f_Z$ is fixed.
\end{proof}

Since $\overline{\mathsf E}$, with the conversion at $Z$, is closed,
$\delta(H_M\overline{\mathsf E})=(N_Z-N_M)\overline{\mathsf E}$ by
\eqref{eq:compose-homotopy}. With
$B=B_{\rm cut}+H_M\overline{\mathsf E}$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:neck-homotopy}
 N_Z\overline{\mathsf E}-F^Z_{\lambda_0}=\delta B .
\end{equation}

\subsection{The mixed excision theorem}
\label{subsec:mixed-excision}
\begin{theorem}[Mixed excision]\label{thm:mixed-excision}
The mixed map $N_Z$ of the folded pair $(A_0,A_1)$, composed with the
reverse excision map $\overline{\mathsf E}$, is chain homotopic over $\ZZ$
to $(-1)^t$ times the tensor product $N_-\otimes^{\mathrm r}N_+$ of the two separate
mixed maps, once the target of $N_Z$ is identified with the tensor product
of their targets by the closed isomorphism $\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}$. The homotopy is
an explicit sum of signed counts. Precisely, with the conversion at the
closed cut placed as in Convention~\ref{conv:interior-line} and
$\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}$ as in \eqref{eq:KR-def},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-explicit}
 (-1)^t\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}N_Z\overline{\mathsf E}-N_-\otimes^{\mathrm r}N_+=\delta H,
 \qquad
 H=P-\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}B_J+\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}B .
\end{equation}
Each term of $H$ is a signed count of isolated parametrized solutions in
a one-parameter family, or its composite or right tensor product with
closed counts and with $\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}$. Here $\overline{\mathsf E}$ is the
reverse excision map at the closed-cut data of
Proposition~\ref{prop:closed-neck}; by Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing}(c)
it is chain homotopic to the map at the data of that proposition. In
particular
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-reverse}
 \mathcal K^{\mathrm r}N_Z\overline{\mathsf E}\simeq(-1)^t(N_-\otimes^{\mathrm r}N_+).
\end{equation}
For $z\in C_G(Z)$ in the structure of $\overline{\mathcal E}$, write
$N_Zz$ for $N_Z\Theta^{\mathrm r}z$, as in the composite
$N_Z\overline{\mathsf E}$. Then for a homogeneous cycle $z$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-cycle}
 [\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}N_Zz]=\pm[(N_-\otimes^{\mathrm r}N_+)\mathsf Ez].
\end{equation}
The gauge complexes of $(-M)^\#$ and $(M^+)^\#$ carry the orientations
defined by their reference almost-complex structures.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
By \eqref{eq:compose-homotopy} and Proposition~\ref{prop:KR-closed},
$\delta(\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}B)=(-1)^t\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}\delta B$. Adding this, applied
to \eqref{eq:neck-homotopy}, to \eqref{eq:width-homotopy} gives
\eqref{eq:excision-explicit}, and \eqref{eq:excision-reverse} is its
restatement. For \eqref{eq:excision-cycle} apply
\eqref{eq:excision-reverse} to $\mathsf Ez$. After the continuation
homotopy of Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing}(c), the capped comparison
gives $[\overline{\mathsf E}\mathsf Ez]=\pm[z]$ for this homogeneous
class, with one sign (Proposition~\ref{prop:integral-excision}), and applying
$\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}N_Z\Theta^{\mathrm r}$ gives the claim.
\end{proof}

The sign $(-1)^t$ in \eqref{eq:excision-reverse} refers to the fixed
choices of ordinary orientations, of the orientation of $Q$ chosen in
Section~\ref{subsec:torus-lines} and of
the reference comparison \eqref{eq:K0}. Changing one of these choices
changes the identity by one overall sign; no choice changes individual
matrix entries.

Equality~\eqref{eq:excision-cycle} is equality of homology classes of
tensors. If the tensors differ by a boundary, applying the chain
isomorphism~\eqref{eq:beta} produces a boundary in the Hom complex.
This is the mechanism by which the cup formula of Proposition~\ref{prop:cup-formula} becomes a chain homotopy. No inference from equality of induced maps on
homology is involved.

\section{The compression cup before reflection}\label{sec:cup}

This section proves the cup formula, Proposition~\ref{prop:cup-formula}:
the compression cup, before a reflection pairing is chosen, is
chain homotopic, up to one sign, to the composite of the transposed
reflected mixed map of Section~\ref{subsec:cup-formula}, the gauge
one-handle map and the mixed map of the upper presentation. The two
inputs are
the oriented folded triangle comparison, Lemma~\ref{lem:folded-triangle},
and mixed excision, Theorem~\ref{thm:mixed-excision}, applied to the
gauge invariant of the one-handle filling.

\subsection{The tensor supplied by the folded triangle}
We use the one-handle geometry and notation of
Section~\ref{subsec:handle-geometry}. A presentation $p$ of $M$ has
torus compression bodies $U_0,U_1$ of genus $h\ge2$, the compression
body $C=C_{h,g}$ with $g=h+k$ adds $k$ handles, $V_i=C\circ U_i$, and
$M^+=M\#^k(S^1\times S^2)$. The correspondence $L_C$, the height
functions $\zeta_i$, the height Lagrangians $R_i$ at height $t$, the
lower Lagrangians $\widetilde P_i$, the product $\mathbb X$ with
$A_i=\widetilde P_i^-\times R_i$, and the incidence conditions on
$(A_0,L_C,A_1)$ are as defined there.

The upper data may carry generic corrections $\gamma$ supported away
from the critical sets of the height functions. These are taken in a closed ball $|\gamma|\le\delta(t)$
in the span of a finite family. On this ball the corrected
Lagrangians remain height Lagrangians at height $t$, and the
restriction map of the family of perturbed flat connections
parametrized by the primary perturbations remains a submersion
\cite[Section~2.3]{DFL}. Hence the incidence conditions
hold for generic corrections, as in Paper~I, Definition~11.12, and
the paragraph following it. The resulting upper presentation is
the upper height presentation of Definition~\ref{def:fold-pair}.

Fix the height $t$. Choose an open $C^1$-neighbourhood
$\mathcal N_C(t)$ of the pair $(L(U_0),L(U_1))$, together with the
corresponding neighbourhood of the reflected pair, with the
following property: for every lower pair in $\mathcal N_C(t)$ and
every correction in the ball, the two auxiliary height intersections
remain transverse, and their critical points continue uniquely from
the product Morse model. Such a neighbourhood exists by openness of
transversality for these compact intersections and compactness of
the ball of corrections. For $k=0$ we shrink it so that it lies in
the neighbourhood $\mathcal N(t)$ of \ref{I5} at the same height. For
$k\ge1$ the upper Lagrangians $R_i$ are fixed, and the lower primary
data are in addition required to lie in the neighbourhood
$\mathcal N_{C,R}$ of Paper~I, Lemma~11.7. In both cases the grading,
action and coefficient statements of Paper~I, Lemma~11.7, hold for
the lower data. The lower data are small presentations
(Section~\ref{subsec:admissible}) whose Lagrangians lie in
$\mathcal N_C(t)$.

The two auxiliary complexes are
$\CF_{\mathbb X}(A_0,L_C)$ and $\CF_{\mathbb X}(L_C,A_1)$. Their
distinguished top cycles are $q_0^*$ and $q_1^*$, respectively. Top
degree refers to the auxiliary integer grading $m$ of Paper~I,
Section~11.3, whose differences satisfy
$m(a)-m(x)=\ind(u)-8\kappa_{\mathrm{aux}}(u)$.
Here $\kappa_{\mathrm{aux}}$ is the relative class of Paper~I's
auxiliary pair. Let $\mathcal A_i$ denote its critical action: the
values at the intersection points of the local exact-graph primitive
of the perturbed pair, with the sign fixed by the ordered pair.
Writing $\omega_{\mathbb X}$ for the symplectic form of $\mathbb X$,
the relation is
\[
4\pi^2\kappa_{\mathrm{aux}}(u)
 =\int u^*\omega_{\mathbb X}-\bigl(\mathcal A_i(a)-\mathcal A_i(x)\bigr).
\]
At the flat height data, $\mathcal A_i$ is the height function
$t\zeta_i$ with the sign of the ordered pair. Under small changes of
the primary data, $\mathcal A_i$ is the primitive of the changed pair,
not the function $t\zeta_i$. Thus the symplectic area includes an
endpoint correction; its quotient by $4\pi^2$ is the total
topological energy. Attaching a positive generator sphere increases
$\kappa_{\mathrm{aux}}$ by $1/2$ and the index by $4$, leaving
$m(a)-m(x)$ unchanged. The normalization of $m$ agrees with the Morse
grading. A top class is defined by $m$, not by the parity grading.
Depending on the order of the pair, the corresponding critical point
is the maximum or minimum of the height function.

Denote the upper height presentation by $q=C\circ p$; this notation
includes its height perturbation. With a split almost-complex
structure on $\mathbb X$, folding identifies the output complex with
$\CF(\bar p)\otimes^{\mathrm r}\CF(q)$. Let $\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}$ be the closed
identification \eqref{eq:KR-def} of Section~\ref{sec:excision}, with
its transported orientation lines. It uses the orientation system
transported independently at $\bar p$; the reflection pairing with
$\CF(p)^\vee$ is constructed in Section~\ref{sec:reflection}. The
triangle product for the ordered triple $(A_0,L_C,A_1)$, including
the conversion $\Theta$ at its closed gauge cut $Z$ in the position of
Convention~\ref{conv:interior-line}, is denoted by
$\mu_{\mathbb X}^\Theta$. It gives the cycle
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cup-tensor}
 z_C=\mathcal K^{\mathrm r}\mu_{\mathbb X}^\Theta(q_0^*\otimes^{\mathrm r} q_1^*)
       \in\CF(\bar p)\otimes^{\mathrm r}\CF(q).
\end{equation}

\begin{definition}\label{def:fold-pair}
Fix a height $t$, the ball of corrections $|\gamma|\le\delta(t)$ and
the neighbourhood $\mathcal N_C(t)$ above.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item The \emph{upper height presentation} at height $t$ has the
compression bodies $V_0,V_1$, the Lagrangians $R_0,R_1$, possibly
corrected by some $\gamma$ in the ball, and its height and auxiliary
regular data (Paper~I, Lemma~11.3).
\item Let $p$ be lower data as above (small, with Lagrangians in $\mathcal N_C(t)$ and, for $k\ge1$, primary data in $\mathcal N_{C,R}$) which satisfies the incidence conditions of Section~\ref{subsec:handle-geometry} with
the upper height presentation $q=C\circ p$. The \emph{unpaired
compression cup} is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:check-cup}
 \check U_C=\operatorname{bend}_{\CF(\bar p)}(z_C):
       \CF(\bar p)^\vee\longrightarrow\CF(q),
\end{equation}
where $\operatorname{bend}$ is the bending map of
Section~\ref{sec:algebra} and $z_C$ is the cycle~\eqref{eq:cup-tensor}.
\item Let $k=0$, so that $L_C$ is the diagonal, and write $r$ for the
upper height presentation. A \emph{small fold pair} $(p,r)$ at
height $t$ consists of a small lower presentation $p$ with
Lagrangians in $\mathcal N_C(t)$ and an upper height presentation $r$
at height $t$ whose primary data lie in the ball $\mathfrak B$ of
Section~\ref{sec:characters}. The pair is subject to the incidence
conditions and the auxiliary regularity conditions above (see also
Section~\ref{subsec:admissible}). For such a pair put
$B(p,r)=\check U_C$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

Thus a fold pair is a particular pair of nearby presentations with a
fixed diagonal triangle and its two height caps. Unfolded along the
seam, the folded triangle is a quilted triangle whose seam carries
the correspondence $L_C$. Paper~I, Remark~11.6, relates the mod-two
cup to the quilted invariant of $L_C$; that comparison is not used
here.

Let $Z_0=D_0\cup(-C)$ and $Z_1=C\cup(-D_1)$ be the gauge inputs of the auxiliary pairs $(A_0,L_C)$ and $(L_C,A_1)$, each diffeomorphic to $V_i\cup(-V_i)$; let $N_0,N_1$ be their integral mixed maps, and let $\theta_i\in C_G(Z_i)$ be the relative invariant of the auxiliary filling $F_i=V_i\times[0,1]$ of $Z_i$ (Paper~I, Lemma~11.1(i) and Lemma~11.2). The top cycles are normalized by
$q_i^*=\pm N_i\theta_i$. The sign is one sign
of each distinguished cycle. The auxiliary
complexes have the standard filtration. The
unit-diagonal calculation in
Proposition~\ref{prop:N-isomorphism} gives the
coefficient $\pm1$ on $q_i^*$, and the other
coefficients vanish by the grading argument of
Paper~I, Lemma~9.2 and Proposition~9.4, with
Lemma~11.7 for the folded pairs, which uses no
signs. This is an integral normalization, not
merely a mod-two one.

\subsection{The relative invariant of the one-handle filling}
Let $B_k$ be the gauge filling whose closed
boundary is the three-manifold $Z=D_0\cup(-D_1)$ of
Section~\ref{subsec:handle-geometry}, obtained from the folded
triangle and its two auxiliary fillings. Its
relative invariant is a homogeneous cycle
$\psi_{B_k}\in C_G(Z)$. Choose on this filling the almost-complex structure of Section~\ref{subsec:excision-structures}, which satisfies~\eqref{eq:cut-Chern}, and use its restrictions on the pieces. Let
$\Theta'$ be the right-placed conversion at $Z$
from the restriction of this structure to the
restriction of $J_{\overline{\mathcal E}}$ (Section~\ref{subsec:excision-structures}), the structure in which \eqref{eq:excision-cycle} is
stated, and write $\mathsf E\psi_{B_k}$ for
$\mathsf E\Theta'\psi_{B_k}$. Since a composite
of conversions agrees with the direct conversion
up to one sign (Section~\ref{sec:gauge}),
$N_Z\Theta^{\mathrm r}\psi_{B_k}$, with the
conversion of Lemma~\ref{lem:folded-triangle},
agrees up to one sign with
$N_Z\Theta^{\mathrm r}\Theta'\psi_{B_k}$, with the
conversion of \eqref{eq:excision-cycle}. After
excision the resulting relative invariant
is a cycle on
$C_G(-M)\otimes^{\mathrm r} C_G(M^+)$. It is the relative invariant of
the bent cobordism $W_k^\#$: by Paper~I,
Lemma~11.1(ii), $B_k\cup_Z\mathcal E\cong W_k^\#$
with the incoming end read as outgoing and with
lifting group $\phi_{W_k}$. Gluing at the connected
cut $Z$ has no extra gluing fibre
(Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-multiplicity}), and the orbit
count~\eqref{eq:orbit-count} gives the integral
coefficients.

Reading the incoming end of the one-handle
cobordism $W_k$ as an outgoing end, that is,
bending it, gives the identity
\begin{equation}\label{eq:handle-state}
 (\Phi_{G,M}\otimes^{\mathrm r}1)[\mathsf E\psi_{B_k}]
       =\pm[\ten(\IG(W_k))].
\end{equation}
To check the sign convention, let $\varsigma$ be the
relative invariant of the product cylinder with both ends bent
outgoing. Gluing the doubly incoming cylinder of
Section~\ref{sec:gauge} to it along $-M$ gives the
product cylinder. In ordered determinant gluing the
line of $x\in C_G(M)$ passes the $-M$ factor of
$\varsigma=\sum\varsigma_1\otimes\varsigma_2$, so the glued map is
$x\mapsto\sum(-1)^{|x||\varsigma_1|}\langle\varsigma_1,x\rangle_M\varsigma_2$,
and it is $\pm1$. By the definition of $\Phi_{G,M}$,
$\langle y,x\rangle_M=\ev'(\Phi_{G,M}y\otimes x)$, where
$\ev'(f\otimes x)=(-1)^{|f|}f(x)$ is the closed
evaluation for~\eqref{eq:tensor}. The same composite
formed with $\ev'$ and $\ten(1)$ is the identity, so
$(\Phi_{G,M}\otimes1)\varsigma=\pm\ten(1)$, with one sign. Glue $W_k$ to its second
end and use the ordered determinant gluing.
This gives the tensor of the right-placed
map. An odd endpoint conversion is already
part of that map through
\eqref{eq:theta-grading}; moving it to the
other side would change the tensor by a
grading operator. The residual signs in
\eqref{eq:handle-state} are therefore scalar.
The same argument holds for $k=0$, when the
cobordism map is gauge continuation.

\subsection{The cup formula}\label{subsec:cup-formula}
Define the transposed reflected mixed map
\begin{equation}\label{eq:n-p}
 n_p=\ev_{C_G(M)}^{-1}
       (N_{\bar p}\Phi_{G,M}^{-1})^\vee:
       \CF(\bar p)^\vee\longrightarrow C_G(M).
\end{equation}
It is an even chain isomorphism when the
reference almost-complex structures of $M$ and
$-M$ are paired and the gauge end data at $\bar p$
are the reflections of those at $p$, as for the
bent product cylinder defining $\Phi_{G,M}$. The
remaining regularizing data of the mixed domain
defining $N_{\bar p}$ are chosen independently, as in Paper~I,
Section~11.2. Other gauge end data at $\bar p$
change $N_{\bar p}$ by the gauge continuation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:end-data}(i), and $n_p$ is
then an even chain homotopy equivalence. The
definition uses only gauge duality and the mixed
map at the reflected presentation.

The integral version of the mod-two folded triangle comparison~\ref{I4} is the folded form of
the triangle comparison \eqref{eq:triangle-square}.

\begin{lemma}[Folded triangle comparison]\label{lem:folded-triangle}
Let $N_0$, $N_1$ be the integral mixed maps of the product-target pairs
$(A_0,L_C)$ and $(L_C,A_1)$, with gauge inputs $Z_0=D_0\cup(-C)$ and
$Z_1=C\cup(-D_1)$, and let $\mu_{\mathscr W}:C_G(Z_0)\otimes^{\mathrm r} C_G(Z_1)\to
C_G(Z)$ be the integral gauge map of the cobordism formed by the pieces
$D_0$, $C$, $D_1$. With the conversion at $Z$ placed as in
Convention~\ref{conv:interior-line},
\[
 \mu_{\mathbb X}^\Theta(N_0\otimes^{\mathrm r}N_1)\simeq\pm N_Z\Theta^{\mathrm r}\mu_{\mathscr W}.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The unsigned argument is the proof of Paper~I, Theorem~11.8: the family
of data of Paper~I, Lemma~9.12, collapses the middle compression body
$C$, which is an odd compression body with Lagrangian $L_C$, stretches the
two ordinary necks cutting off the domains defining the mixed maps of $(A_0,L_C)$ and
$(L_C,A_1)$, and stretches the closed cut $Z$. We orient every
configuration in the family by the several-input construction of
Section~\ref{sec:determinants}, with the conversion line at $Z$ in its
fixed position. At the collapsed end, Theorem~\ref{thm:B} identifies the
orientations of the index-zero solutions with the orientations of the
symplectic triangle glued to the mixed domains of the two auxiliary pairs. It applies to this
collapse: Section~\ref{sec:collapse} rests on the collapse analysis of
Paper~I, Sections~6--8, and the proof of Paper~I, Theorem~11.8, supplies its
inputs for the collapse of $C=C_{h,g}$, with product target, both boundary surfaces
matched and the lower sheet reversed, using the two-trace slice space
$H^1(\Sigma_h;\ad)\oplus H^1(\Sigma_g;\ad)$. The sign comparison of
Proposition~\ref{prop:collapse-sign} uses only canonical orientations
along paths of invertible operators, so the reversed sheet does not
enter it. Ordinary gluing along
the two stretched necks (Lemma~\ref{lem:line-gluing}) gives the count
$\mu_{\mathbb X}^\Theta(N_{\mathcal V_0}\otimes^{\mathrm r}N_{\mathcal V_1})$, with the
input lines in cyclic order, where $\mathcal V_0$, $\mathcal V_1$ are the
corner pieces of the zero-scale domain, the one-input mixed pieces for
$(A_0,L_C)$ and $(L_C,A_1)$ attached at its incoming ends.
Likewise the mixed map obtained below at the stretched cut is the count
$N_{\mathcal V_Z}$ on the piece $\mathcal V_Z$ of DFL type for $(A_0,A_1)$ beyond
$Z$. By Lemma~\ref{lem:vertex-id}, the strip actions of these
three pieces are those of the target complexes of $N_0$, $N_1$ and
$N_Z$, and the three counts differ from $N_0$, $N_1$ and $N_Z$ by
boundaries $\delta H$ with $H$ odd. Along the family, the output strip
action is defined through the invertible terminal constant pairs on
the output regions (Lemma~\ref{lem:constant-pairs}). It is constant by
the argument of Corollary~\ref{cor:strip-actions}; this is used at the
breaks below. Since
$\mu_{\mathbb X}^\Theta$ and $\Theta^{\mathrm r}\mu_{\mathscr W}$ are closed,
the identity for tensors of homotopies in Section~\ref{subsec:vertex-id}
and~\eqref{eq:compose-homotopy} reduce the lemma to the same statement
for $N_{\mathcal V_0}$, $N_{\mathcal V_1}$ and $N_{\mathcal V_Z}$. In the
rest of the proof $N_0$, $N_1$ and $N_Z$ denote these three counts. At
the stretched cut, gluing at the connected closed cut $Z$ has no extra
