by~\eqref{eq:right-conversion}, since
$\eps^fG=(-1)^{fg}G\eps^f$. Thus passing from
$F$ to $F^{\mathrm r}$ is compatible with
composition after projectivization. The factor
$(-1)^{fg}$ is scalar, although the grading
operator is applied to each map.

For a strict homotopy
$F-F'=dH+Hd$, where
$|H|=f+1$, the correctly
converted homotopy is
$H\eps^f$. Indeed
\[
 \delta(H\eps^f)
 =dH\eps^f-(-1)^{f+1}H\eps^fd
 =(dH+Hd)\eps^f.
\]
Using $H\eps^{f+1}$ instead
would give the wrong sign.
This calculation is used
when a gauge
composition homotopy is
translated into the Hom
complex convention.

Lastly, if
$F_i-G_i=\delta K_i$ and
the maps are closed,
a homotopy between two
composites is obtained
by replacing one factor
at a time and using
\eqref{eq:compose-homotopy}.
For example, for three
factors one may take
\[
 K_3F_2F_1
 +(-1)^{|G_3|}G_3K_2F_1
 +(-1)^{|G_3|+|G_2|}G_3G_2K_1.
\]
Applying $\delta$ telescopes
to $F_3F_2F_1-G_3G_2G_1$.
This is the chain-level
algebra used in
Lemma~\ref{lem:descent}.

\section{Ordinary integral excision}\label{app:selfgluing}

This appendix contains the ordinary gauge theory used in
Section~\ref{sec:excision}, with the geometry of
Section~\ref{subsec:handle-geometry}.
Section~\ref{subsec:excision-structures} fixes the almost-complex
structures at the closed cut, Section~\ref{subsec:lifting-mult} computes
lifting groups and gluing multiplicities,
Section~\ref{sec:ordinary-selfgluing} proves
Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing}, and Section~\ref{subsec:capped} proves
that the excision maps of Kronheimer and Mrowka are inverse over $\ZZ$ up
to one sign (Proposition~\ref{prop:integral-excision}). The
compactification and gluing arguments are those of
\cite[Section~3.9]{KM-unknot} with empty singular locus. Their definition
of a cut permits a nonseparating cut, and the odd torus satisfies the
non-integral condition required there. The orientation of the self-glued
count is \cite[Proposition~5.5]{KM-unknot}, whose sign is fixed by the
argument of \cite[Lemma~2.4]{KM-sutures}. We give the steps that are
specific to the present cut data, and refer to these sources and
to~\ref{I10} for the rest. The points of these proofs that are not in the
literature are listed at the beginning of Section~\ref{sec:excision}.

\subsection{Almost-complex structures at the closed cut}
\label{subsec:excision-structures}
Let $W_k:M\to M^+$ be the cobordism obtained by adding $k$
one-handles, where $M^+=M\#^k(S^1\times S^2)$. The folded triangle
for this compression has a closed gauge cut
\[
 Z\cong(-M)\#M^+\#T^3.
\]
The odd tori $T_a,T_b\subset Z$ are homologous, and the
lifting group at this cut is generated by
$s_Z=\PD[T_a]\pmod2$. Excision along these tori gives
\[
 Z'=(-M)^\#\sqcup(M^+)^\#.
\]
Write $\mathsf E:C_G(Z)\to C_G(Z')$ for the gauge
excision map and $\overline{\mathsf E}$ for its reverse.
The tensor complex at $Z'$ uses the right convention.

The almost-complex structure induced by the folded
triangle at $Z$ need not equal the one induced by the
mixed torus domain. A conversion at this interior cut
is therefore required. We choose the structures so that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cut-Chern}
 c_1(J)[T_a]\equiv0\pmod4.
\end{equation}
For two such structures, the lifting-class sign~\eqref{eq:gauge-character} of the conversion is trivial on $s_Z$.
Consequently the conversion at $Z$ is defined on the
quotient complex.

The choice uses the topology of the particular fillings. In the
handle filling of Paper~I, Section~11.1, one has
\[
 B_k=(W_k\setminus\operatorname{int}\nu(\gamma))
           \natural(T^2\times D^2),
\]
and $T_a,T_b$ are homologous in $B_k$ to the torus factor of the
displayed cap. Choose the product almost-complex structure on the cap.
Its first Chern class evaluates to zero on the torus factor, hence
every extension of it over $B_k$ evaluates to zero on $T_a$ and $T_b$. The boundary sum is
along a three-ball, so Mayer--Vietoris makes
$H^2(B_k;\ZZ)\to H^2(T^2\times D^2;\ZZ)$ surjective.
The relative obstruction argument of
Lemma~\ref{lem:almost-complex}, with the whole cap as the prescribed
region, extends this product structure: the primary obstruction
has zero connecting class and the secondary relative group in
degree four vanishes. The other end restrictions remain free.

On the excision side choose the same product germ on the
$T^2$ times polygon region of Paper~I, Section~10. The attachments
are away from the fibres at $T_a,T_b$. Write $A=T^2\times U$, where $U$ is the disk polygon of that
construction. Its complement is the disjoint union of
$[0,1]\times Q_-$ and $[0,1]\times Q_+$, with
$Q_\pm=(T^2\times I_\pm)\#M_\pm$, where $M_-=-M$ and $M_+=M^+$. Each $Q_\pm$ has an integral
cohomology class restricting to the torus generator on both its
boundary tori. These classes match the generator on $A$ along
all four attaching faces. Mayer--Vietoris therefore gives
$H^2(\mathcal E;\ZZ)\to H^2(A;\ZZ)$ onto. Also $H^3(A;\ZZ)=0$
and $H^4(\mathcal E;\ZZ)=0$, so the secondary relative obstruction
vanishes. The same argument applies with the cobordism reversed. Make both structures cylindrical at the
closed cut, preserving their product germ near these two fibres.
Thus their Chern evaluations on these fibres are zero, which is
stronger than~\eqref{eq:cut-Chern}.

Two end restrictions are prescribed. By convention the separate torus
domains $W_{\pm,\mathsf R}$ read their input lines $L_G(a_\pm)$ in the
reference almost-complex structures of $(-M)^\#$ and $(M^+)^\#$; this is
unobstructed, since their gauge regions deformation retract onto the
input slice. Choose $J_{\overline{\mathcal E}}$ with these reference
structures as its input
restriction at $Z'$; the two prescriptions agree on the collars of the
tori of $Z'$, since we take these tori to be the tori $T_0$ of the two
objects, where the object germ is the product germ. Then
choose $J_{\mathcal E}$ with restriction $J_{\overline{\mathcal E}}|_Z$ at
$Z$. Both extensions are unobstructed. Write $Q=Q_-\sqcup Q_+$, so that
the complement of $A$ is $[0,1]\times Q$. By excision,
\[
 H^*(\overline{\mathcal E},A\cup\partial_{\rm in}\overline{\mathcal E};\ZZ)
 \cong H^*\bigl((Q,\partial Q)\times([0,1],\{0\});\ZZ\bigr)=0,
\]
and likewise $H^*(\mathcal E,A\cup Z;\ZZ)=0$, so the primary and
secondary relative obstructions vanish. The product germ near the fibres
at $T_a,T_b$ is kept, so~\eqref{eq:cut-Chern} is unaffected. With these
choices the orientation maps of Section~\ref{subsec:torus-maps} and the
maps $N_\pm$ read their input lines in the same structures, and the
composite $\overline{\mathsf E}\mathsf E$ has equal end structures at
$Z$. Condition~\eqref{eq:cut-Chern} is thus a property of the chosen
structures on the stated fillings and product regions.

\subsection{Lifting groups and gluing multiplicities}
\label{subsec:lifting-mult}
It is useful to perform the gauge excision calculation
before passing to a quotient. Let $\mathcal G_0(X)$
be the subgroup of bundle automorphisms with trivial
lifting class. If a finite group $\phi_X$ acts freely
and orientation preservingly on the relevant
zero-dimensional solution set, its quotient count is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:orbit-count}
 I(X)^{\phi_X}(\bar a_0,\bar a_1)
 =\frac{1}{|\phi_X|}
   \sum_{a_i\in\bar a_i}\#\mathcal M(X;a_0,a_1).
\end{equation}
The numerator is divisible by $|\phi_X|$: it is a
sum of free orbits, each with a constant sign.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:gluing-multiplicity}
Let $X$ be connected and let $Y$ be a closed
hypersurface with cut manifold $X_Y$. Suppose the
critical limits on $Y$ have trivial stabilizer in the
adjoint determinant-one gauge group. For each matched
configuration on $X_Y$ that glues, the resulting
$\mathcal G_0(X)$-classes form a torsor for
\[
 K_Y=\ker\bigl(H^1(X;\ZZ/2)\longrightarrow
                         H^1(X_Y;\ZZ/2)\bigr).
\]
In particular their multiplicity is $|K_Y|$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Choose representatives whose two limits agree under
the fixed gluing identification. Two glued classes whose
restrictions to the cut manifold agree as determinant-one
classes differ by an automorphism whose lifting
class restricts to zero on $X_Y$. Conversely every
such class changes the gluing while leaving the cut
class fixed. The stabilizer condition makes this action
free. Its transitivity follows by comparing the two
gluing identifications on the two copies of $Y$.
The Mayer--Vietoris sequence identifies $K_Y$ with the
image of the connecting map from $H^0(Y;\ZZ/2)$.
The matching and analytic assertions needed for the excision
cobordisms are part of Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing}, which is
proved in Section~\ref{sec:ordinary-selfgluing}.
\end{proof}

A finite lifting group acts freely on the solutions
in the present calculation. Indeed, a fixed solution
would give a parallel automorphism. Its restriction
to an irreducible end must be the identity once its
lifting class there vanishes. A parallel automorphism
which is the identity on an end is the identity
everywhere on a connected cobordism. An element whose
class is nonzero at every end, such as the classes $P_\pm$ of
Section~\ref{subsec:capped}, restricts
to $s_Z$ at the $Z$ ends, and $\phi_Z$ acts freely on the
critical points of $Z$, so it fixes no solution either. The almost-complex
orientation construction preserves signs under the
whole group. Thus~\eqref{eq:orbit-count} applies.

\subsection{Self-gluing along the torus cut}
\label{sec:ordinary-selfgluing}
Write $\mathcal E$ for the cobordism defining $\mathsf E$ and put
$V=\mathcal E\cup_{Z'}\overline{\mathcal E}$. The local polygon
description gives a nonseparating cut $\Sigma=T^2\times\gamma$ of $V$;
its complement $V^\circ$ is connected and retains the original $Z$ ends,
and the bundle is odd on the torus fibre. The other composite
$\overline{\mathcal E}\cup_Z\mathcal E$ has a separating torus cut. All
components of the cobordisms that we regularize have an original
cylindrical end; the caps are kept fixed. On each
new three-torus end we use a fixed flat product metric and the
unperturbed equation. The odd bundle on the three-torus has two
determinant-one flat critical points $g_1,g_2$, both acyclic and
interchanged by $\PD[T^2\times\{\mathrm{point}\}]$. Their relative
grading is four modulo eight, by the proof of
\cite[Proposition~5.2(a)]{KM-unknot}; see also
\cite[Lemma~5.3]{KM-unknot}. Hence they have the same parity, which we
denote by $|g|$. The caps $T^2\times D^2$ of
Section~\ref{subsec:capped} carry the same unperturbed end data and no
secondary perturbation.

Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing} is proved below. Its ordinary parts are
the broken-metric compactification and gluing of
\cite[Section~3.9]{KM-unknot} and the signed self-gluing formula
\cite[Proposition~5.5]{KM-unknot}; the points that are not in the
literature are those listed at the beginning of
Section~\ref{sec:excision}. In the trace the traced factor is placed
last. For a map $F$ of degree $f$ on a tensor end with torus basis $g_j$
and diagonal blocks $F_{jj}$, the right tensor convention then gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ordinary-right-trace}
 \operatorname{Tr}^{\mathrm r}(F)=\sum_j(-1)^{(f+1)|g_j|}F_{jj}.
\end{equation}
Both torus generators have parity $|g|$, so the comparison with caps of
coefficients $u,v\in\{1,-1\}$ carries the factor $(-1)^{(f+1)|g|}/(uv)$,
up to one fixed overall orientation. The gluing multiplicities are those
of Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-multiplicity}: two for the nonseparating cut
$\Sigma$ and for the two-edge composition cycle at $Z'$, and one for a
separating cut or a tree of caps.

\begin{proof}[Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing}]
\emph{The three-torus ends.} Keep the entire ordinary end functional
$\mathrm{CS}_{\mathfrak h}+f$ of Paper~I fixed on the original ends,
including its trajectory perturbation; only the secondary cobordism
perturbations vary. On each new three-torus end use the fixed flat
product metric and the unperturbed equation, throughout all comparison
families. The two critical points $g_1,g_2$ of
Section~\ref{sec:ordinary-selfgluing} have the same parity, and the
required lifting action on them is free.

Every nonconstant unperturbed torus trajectory has full index at least
four. Indeed, the two end Hessians are conjugate by an adjoint gauge
transformation, so their spectral correction terms agree. The cylinder
index and energy are $8\kappa$ and $8\pi^2\kappa$, respectively, with
$\kappa\in\frac12\ZZ$, and positive energy therefore implies index at
least four. The compactifications used have total dimension at most
two. After joint regularization of the cut pieces, a torus trajectory of
index at least four would leave a negative index for the remaining
pieces, counting the data parameter once, so such a stratum is empty.
Hence no torus trajectory perturbation is needed in this dimension
range, and regularity of the unperturbed torus trajectories is never
used.

\emph{Collar-supported secondary perturbations.} Choose a product collar
joining each component to an autonomous end, and a compact interval $J$
in that collar. The metric is fixed there, including in parameter
families. Let $F_Y$ be the image on the collar cross-section $Y$ of the
allowed lifting group. The secondary functionals are
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ordinary-secondary}
 q_s(B)=\sum_\ell c_\ell\psi_\ell(s)\mathfrak f_\ell(B),
 \qquad \operatorname{supp}\psi_\ell\subset J,
\end{equation}
where the $\mathfrak f_\ell$ are $F_Y$-invariant holonomy cylinder
functions on $Y$ whose holonomy loops lie in a single slice. Elements of
the kernel of the restriction to $F_Y$ impose no further condition. A
countable dense collection of profiles and cylinder functions, with a
weighted Banach completion controlling all required smooth norms, gives
the secondary perturbations of \cite[Section~3.7]{KM-unknot} with sums of
compact time profiles. The holonomy estimates of
\cite[Propositions~3.7 and~3.15]{KM-instantons} persist: time derivatives
multiply the corresponding profile bounds, and the derivative in $B$ is
bounded on $L^2$ on bounded sets of data. For the compactness estimate,
include the fixed primary cutoff terms in $q_s$. In a consistent
gradient normalization,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ordinary-energy}
 \frac{d}{ds}(\mathrm{CS}+q_s)(B(s))
 =-\|\dot B(s)\|^2+\partial_s q_s(B(s)),
\end{equation}
and the additional integral is bounded by the $L^1$ norm of the time
derivatives of the coefficients, uniformly under stretching. The spatial
estimates, local regularity and the autonomous middle-neck equation are
those of the ordinary broken-metric construction. No perturbation
depends on connections on both sides of a cut.

\emph{Invariant regularity.} Regularity is the transversality of
\cite[Propositions~3.8 and~3.10]{KM-unknot} for secondary perturbations,
carried out invariantly under the lifting groups as in~\ref{I10}
(Paper~I, Theorem~5.6), here with collar-supported time profiles. Three
points are specific to this class.

First, slice continuation. On a compact slab of a collar, the equation
for a difference of two solutions or for an adjoint field has the form
$(\partial_s+H(s)+K(s))u=0$, where $H$ is formally self-adjoint on a
common smooth domain, $H'$ and $K$ are bounded on $L^2$, and
$H=\pm *d_{B(s)}$ for the one-form equation; the difference of two holonomy terms has this form by integration of their derivatives along the segment between the two connections. Expanding
$\|(\partial_s+H+\lambda\varphi')v\|^2$ for compactly supported $v$ and
integrating by parts gives, for a concave weight $\varphi$ with
$-\varphi''\ge c>0$ and large $\lambda$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ordinary-continuation}
 (c\lambda-C)\|e^{-\lambda\varphi}u\|^2
 \le 2\|e^{-\lambda\varphi}(\partial_s+H+K)u\|^2 .
\end{equation}
Choose $\varphi$ increasing on a short slab. If a solution vanishes before a slice, cut it off farther into the slab; the cutoff error has strictly larger $\varphi$ than a neighbourhood of the slice, so letting $\lambda$ tend to infinity proves vanishing in that neighbourhood. Reversing $s$ gives the other direction. Vanishing on a whole slice suffices, since the equation then makes all time derivatives vanish there. The estimate uses only the symmetry of $H$.

Second, freeness and invariant density. If an allowed automorphism fixes
a slice of a solution, then by invariance and slice continuation it fixes
the whole end and its limiting critical point; freeness of $F_Y$ there
forces its lifting class to vanish, and irreducibility makes its even
lift central. So each collar slice has a free $F_Y$ orbit. Prescribing
equivariant first jets on a finite-dimensional slice of small radius and its finitely many disjoint
translates, approximating by cylinder functions as in the proof of
\cite[Proposition~3.10]{KM-instantons}, and averaging, one obtains
invariant cylinder functions that detect every direction transverse to gauge. Freeness ensures that the prescribed derivative survives the averaging.

Third, the cokernel. A cokernel element $(\xi,b)$ of $(d_A^*,L_A)$, with
$L_A$ the linearized perturbed ASD operator, has $\xi=0$ by gauge
equivariance and the $L^2$ end condition. On the collar write $b$ by a
one-form $y(s)$ with $d_{B(s)}^*y=0$. If the cokernel annihilates every
secondary variation, then time localization, invariant density and the
Coulomb condition give $y=0$ on $J$. Then \eqref{eq:ordinary-continuation},
local unique continuation on the unperturbed core and slice continuation
on the other collars give $b=0$. All nonlocal terms lie in the product
end collars, where slice continuation applies.

Sard--Smale then gives invariant regular data. For a broken configuration
with pieces indexed by $i$ and one parameter $b\in B$, the joint
linearization $((\xi_i)_i,\dot b)\mapsto(D_i\xi_i+\partial_b\mathcal
F_i\dot b)_i$, in which the shared parameter occurs once, is onto because
the variations on the pieces are independent. Choosing regular data on
fixed faces first, extending over their collars using parameter-dependent variations supported away from them, and intersecting the
residual sets for the finitely many cut patterns and countably many
relative classes gives the relative regular families of
\cite[Proposition~3.10 and Section~3.9]{KM-unknot} in the invariant
class. Fixed regular caps require no variation.

\emph{Matching on the prescribed bundle and finiteness.} An even gauge
transformation is a section of the bundle with fibre $\SU(2)$ and
conjugation structure action; no lift of the underlying $\SO(3)$ bundle
is required. The fibre is $2$-connected and conjugation acts trivially
on $\pi_3$, so if gauge values are prescribed on boundary components
$B_0$ of a compact cobordism $W$, the only extension obstruction lies in
\[
 H^4(W,B_0;\ZZ)\cong H_0(W,\partial W\setminus B_0;\ZZ).
\]
It vanishes when every component has an unprescribed boundary end.
Apply this to the two new boundaries of $V^\circ$, leaving its original
$Z$ ends free. Equal critical classes can therefore be represented by
equal connections under the prescribed gluing identification, with
allowed even changes at the original ends, and they glue on the
specified global bundle. For capping, keep the cap representatives fixed
and make the changes on $V^\circ$.

Relative homotopy classes with a fixed tuple of critical end classes form
a $\ZZ$-torsor whose generator changes the index by eight \cite[Sections~3.2 and~3.7]{KM-unknot}, and each class
is represented on the fixed bundle in this nonsingular setting
\cite[Section~3.7]{KM-unknot}. A fixed index and tuple therefore allow at
most one relative class. The critical sets are finite, and in a regular
family each component index is bounded below by minus the base
dimension, so at fixed total dimension only finitely many index
allocations occur; the shared parameter is counted once.

The final secondary data may be taken to be finite sums in
\eqref{eq:ordinary-secondary}. On a bounded neighbourhood of data, \eqref{eq:ordinary-energy}, the index-energy relation and the class calculation make the index ranges in question finite. Regularity is open on the finite union of the low-dimensional compactifications, which have regular gluing neighbourhoods at their broken strata: a sequence of nonregular solutions for convergent data would converge to a regular point or to a regular broken configuration, contradicting the local estimate. Finite sums are dense, also for
smooth coefficient families on a compact base, and approximating within
this open set, relative to the prescribed face collars, preserves the
original end equations and all supports.

\emph{Gluing and compactness.} Gluing is the ordinary linear and
nonlinear gluing of \cite[Section~3.9]{KM-unknot}. The point specific to
self-gluing is that a regular index-zero cut solution $A$ has a single
invertible operator $D_A$ on the connected cut piece, including both
stubs. Its inverse is patched with the uniform inverse of
$\partial_s+B_\beta$ on the cylinder, where $B_\beta$ is the invertible
extended Hessian at the irreducible nondegenerate limit $\beta$.
Compactly supported secondary terms and slice-local primary terms
commute with the cutoffs, and stabilization gives the same charts and
determinant maps for families that are regular as parametrized
families.

In the compactness argument of \cite[Section~3.9]{KM-unknot}, the index
exclusion above replaces regularity of all trajectories on the new torus
ends: any nonconstant torus trajectory removes at least four from the
index available to the regular joint cut operator, which is impossible
also in the two-parameter corner families. The retained strata have
regular original-end trajectories, regular joint cut operators and
acyclic matching limits, which are the inputs of the local compactness
and gluing arguments. In total dimension at most one, the cut solutions
form the broken-metric boundary; further neck
breaking has higher codimension, and bubbling is excluded by
low-dimensional compactness. The local gluing charts give its collars,
and finiteness of the union over relative classes gives one neck
threshold: additional solutions at lengths tending to infinity would
converge to a boundary point and hence lie in its gluing collar. For disjoint cuts use the multi-parameter corner model, whose
two-parameter index-zero family has dimension two; a simultaneous
stretching of two components is the diagonal in the two gluing
parameters, so the two-edge composition cycle is obtained by one
separating gluing followed by one self-gluing. The cuts $\Sigma$ and
$Z'$ intersect, so their comparison joins finite stretching parameters
by a relative regular path instead of stretching both cuts at once.

\emph{The lifting fibre.} Compare the even lifts on the components of a
cut manifold. Their values at paired ends agree up to central signs, and
changing a lift by the centre on one component changes the incident
signs by the graph coboundary. The remaining choices form the first
cohomology of the cut graph over $\FF$, identified with $K_Y$ by
Mayer--Vietoris. These classes are realized by gauge transformations.
For a connected cut, choose a unit section $n$ of the adjoint bundle on
$Y$ and, on its normal collar, use the adjoint of
\[
 \cos(\pi t)+\sin(\pi t)n,\qquad 0\le t\le1,
\]
with $t$ constant near the endpoints. The section exists because the
Euler class of an oriented rank-three bundle is $2$-torsion and
$H^3(Y;\ZZ)$ is torsion-free. The adjoint transformation is the identity
at both ends, while its lift runs from $+1$ to $-1$, so its lifting class
is $\PD[Y]$. The action is transitive by comparison of the lifts, and
free: a fixed solution would give a parallel automorphism that is even
on an original irreducible end, hence the identity there and everywhere.
The almost-complex determinant orientations preserve this action. Thus
the multiplicities are two for the nonseparating cut $\Sigma$ and the
two-edge composition cycle at $Z'$, and one for a separating cut or a
tree of caps.

\emph{Trace signs and homotopies.} Use the induced almost-complex
structures at the ends, with the orientation torsors of
\cite[Section~5.1]{KM-unknot}, and apply
\cite[Proposition~5.5]{KM-unknot} with the enlarged group exactly
$K_\Sigma=\langle\PD[\Sigma]\rangle$. This group restricts trivially to
$V^\circ$, to $\Sigma$ and to the original ends, so the trace uses the
full determinant-one complex on $\Sigma$. The free orientation-preserving
action just proved recovers the raw count as twice that trace. The larger
quotient is taken only after this step. The alternating sign is the analytic determinant sign given by the argument of \cite[Lemma~2.4]{KM-sutures}, the parity of
spectral flow in the periodic model, so there is no independent sign
choice for each generator or relative class.

For $f=0$, \eqref{eq:ordinary-right-trace} is this alternating trace.
Moving the traced line past a map of degree $f$ supplies the additional
factor $(-1)^{f|g_j|}$, and conjugation by the tensor isomorphism $T$ of
Section~\ref{sec:algebra} gives the same formula algebraically. The trace
commutes with the Hom differential and satisfies
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}^{\mathrm r}((\Lambda_0\otimes^{\mathrm r}1)F(\Lambda_1\otimes^{\mathrm r}1)) =\Lambda_0\operatorname{Tr}^{\mathrm r}(F)\Lambda_1,
\]
so the external conversion maps can be inserted with their degrees. Since
both torus generators have parity $|g|$, the comparison with unit cap
coefficients $u,v$ has the factor $(-1)^{(f+1)|g|}/(uv)$ stated
after~\eqref{eq:ordinary-right-trace}, up to the fixed overall
orientation. Interior conversions follow
Convention~\ref{conv:interior-line}.

Finally use the parameter-first orientation of
Section~\ref{sec:determinants}, with outward-normal-first boundary
orientations. The source convention in \cite[Section~3.9]{KM-unknot} is
fibre-first. For an $r$-parameter family the permutation of the
determinant factors contributes $(-1)^{r\operatorname{ind}D}$. For a
zero-dimensional one-parameter count the vertical index is $-1$, so this
changes the sign of the count. For the one-dimensional parametrized
moduli space the vertical index is zero, so its orientation and its
endpoint maps are unchanged. For two parameters the permutation sign is
one, and boundary faces use the same one-parameter rule.

It remains to include the generator-order conversion. At the induced end
gradings the unconverted cobordism maps $F_i$ are even. Let $d_0$ and
$H_0$ be the source differential and its fibre-first homotopy count, so
that the oriented boundary formula is $F_1-F_0=d_0H_0+H_0d_0$. Placing an
odd trajectory determinant after its input generator changes $d_0$ to
$d=d_0\eps$ and $H_0$ to $H_0\eps$, and the parameter-first change
contributes one further minus sign. The homotopy operator in our
convention is therefore
\[
 K=-H_0\eps,\qquad
 dK+Kd=d_0H_0+H_0d_0=F_1-F_0.
\]
Here $\eps$ is the grading operator on the appropriate source complex;
the calculation uses that $d_0$ and $H_0$ are odd. On disconnected ends
the whole ordered input generator orientation line gives the tensor
rule~\eqref{eq:tensor}, so the parametrized boundary formula agrees with
\eqref{eq:family-boundary}. Arbitrary external conversions, including
odd ones, are then governed by the Hom Leibniz rule. All these choices
are uniform across matrix entries. This proves all three assertions in
the stated orientation convention.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Excision and reverse excision are inverse over the integers}
\label{subsec:capped}
Let $V=\mathcal E\cup_{Z'}\overline{\mathcal E}$ be
the composite of excision and reverse excision.
The standard local model is $T^2$ times the doubled
excision polygon. It contains a nonseparating
hypersurface $\Sigma=T^2\times\gamma$. Cut along
$\Sigma$ to obtain $V^\circ$, and cap the two new
boundary components by $T^2\times D^2$. The capped
cobordism $V'$ is a product on $Z$; this is the
geometric comparison in the proof of
\cite[Theorem~5.6]{KM-unknot}. As there, give $V$ the
structure $J_V$ which is the product structure on $T^2$
times the doubled excision polygon and which, on the
complementary region $[0,2]\times Q$, is translation
invariant and equal to $J_{\mathcal E}|_Z=J_{\overline{\mathcal E}}|_Z$.
The two agree along $[0,2]\times\partial Q$, because this
structure has the product germ near $T_a$ and $T_b$. Thus
$J_V$ is cylindrical with the product germ near $\Sigma$,
and its restrictions to the two ends $Z$ are those of
$\mathsf E$ and $\overline{\mathsf E}$. The trace of
Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing}(b) is taken with
$J_V|_{V^\circ}$, and $V'$ carries $J_V|_{V^\circ}$ together
with the product structure on the caps. For the other
composite the glued structure
$J_{\overline{\mathcal E}}\cup_ZJ_{\mathcal E}$ already has
this form near its torus cut.

The determinant-one complex on $\Sigma$ has two
critical points $g_1,g_2$. The class
$a=\PD_\Sigma[T^2]$ interchanges them. They must be
kept separate when the caps are attached: the caps
carry the trivial lifting group, since $a$ does not
extend across their meridional disks.

Write $s=\PD_Z[T_a]\pmod2$. In $H^1(V^\circ;\ZZ/2)$
let $\hat s$ be its pullback, and let $m_1,m_2$ be the meridional
classes of the two removed cap neighbourhoods. The
restrictions are
\[
\begin{array}{c|cccc}
 &Z_{\rm in}&Z_{\rm out}&\Sigma_{\rm in}&\Sigma_{\rm out}\\\hline
 \hat s&s&s&0&0\\
 m_1&s&0&a&0\\
 m_2&s&0&0&a\\
 m_1+m_2&0&0&a&a
\end{array}
\]
The group $H^\circ=\langle \hat s,m_1,m_2\rangle$ has order
$8$. It contains both groups needed on the cut
cobordism, but is not itself the pullback of either
of the groups on $V$ and $V'$.

More precisely, $\phi_V$ has order $8$, its image on
$V^\circ$ is $\langle \hat s,m_1+m_2\rangle$ of order $4$,
and its kernel is $K_\Sigma$ of order $2$. The capped
product has group $\phi_{V'}=\langle \hat s\rangle$ of
order $2$. To check these assertions, the two sides
of the excision polygon give classes $P_+,P_-$ and
the disconnected intermediate cut gives a class $c$.
Their restrictions are $\hat s,\hat s,m_1+m_2$, respectively.
They are independent on $V$, as seen by loops
crossing each of the two polygon sides and the
intermediate cut. Their sum $P_++P_-$ is dual to
$\Sigma$, generates the restriction kernel, and is
nonzero because $\Sigma$ is nonseparating.

Choose invariant regularizing data on $V^\circ$.
Let
\[
 D(\alpha,\sigma;\beta,\tau)
 =\#\mathcal M(V^\circ;(\alpha,\sigma),(\beta,\tau)),
\]
where $\sigma,\tau\in\{g_1,g_2\}$. Put
$\Delta(\alpha,\beta)=D(\alpha,g_1;\beta,g_1)$.
The action of $m_1+m_2$ makes the two diagonal
blocks equal; the action of $m_2$ identifies the
off-diagonal block with $\Delta(s\alpha,\beta)$.
Thus, after summing over endpoint lifts, diagonal
and off-diagonal blocks have the same sum.

We verify the cap count for the unperturbed flat product data on the
three-torus ends of Section~\ref{sec:ordinary-selfgluing}. Complete $T^2\times D^2$ by a
product cylindrical end, giving $T^2\times S$, where $S$ is a
complete disk with a cylindrical end. Give $T^2$ a flat metric
and use a product metric on the cap. Let $A_0$ be the pullback
of the odd flat connection on $T^2$. Its adjoint local system
on $T^2$ is acyclic: it splits into the three nontrivial real
sign local systems. The torus Hodge Laplacian therefore has a
positive lower bound in every degree. The product Hodge Laplacian
on $T^2\times S$ has the same lower bound, since its two summands
are nonnegative.

For the full flat operator $D_0=d_{A_0}^*\oplus\sqrt2d_{A_0}^+$,
the identities
\[
 D_0^*D_0=\Delta_{A_0,1},\qquad
 D_0D_0^*=\Delta_{A_0,0}\oplus\Delta_{A_0,2}^+
\]
show that its kernel and cokernel vanish. Elliptic estimates and
the positive gap give an inverse on the cylindrical Sobolev
spaces. Thus the flat cap has index zero and is regular.

Call its boundary lift $g_1$. The other lift $g_2$ has relative
grading four modulo eight, whereas changing a relative class
at a fixed lift changes the cap index by eight. Hence an
index-zero cap can approach only $g_1$, in the zero-charge class
of $A_0$. Its unperturbed ASD energy is $8\pi^2\kappa=0$,
so it is flat. The flat odd torus connection has a unique flat
extension across the disk in the prescribed bundle: the disk
meridian is contractible and $\pi_1(T^2\times S)=\pi_1(T^2)$.
The two boundary lifts distinguish whether the meridional
determinant-one lift extends; only $g_1$ does. Consequently there
is exactly one determinant-one cap class. Its signed coefficient
is a unit, and the reversed cap gives the corresponding dual
unit. This calculation is at the full two-generator torus
complex, before any lifting-group quotient.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:capped}
For the regular data of Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing}, the
quotient counts for $V$ and $V'$ have the same absolute
multiplicity. They agree up to one sign, the same
for all entries.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For $V$, self-gluing has multiplicity $2$ and there
are two equal diagonal blocks. Quotienting by the
group of order $8$ gives
\[
 \frac{2\cdot2}{8}
   \sum_{\alpha\in\bar a,\,\beta\in\bar b}
       \Delta(\alpha,\beta).
\]
For $V'$, the caps have unit coefficients, their
separating gluings have multiplicity $1$, and the
group has order $2$. The result is
\[
 \frac12
   \sum_{\alpha\in\bar a,\,\beta\in\bar b}
       \Delta(\alpha,\beta).
\]
If the second cap selects $g_2$, the symmetry of
the off-diagonal block gives the same sum. The
orientation signs are those in
Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing}; since the unconverted map of
$V^\circ$ is even, they reduce to the factor $(-1)^{|g|}/(uv)$ for every
entry. All group actions preserve the
orientations, so no cancellation occurs inside
an orbit. The two numerical factors agree.
\end{proof}

The fractions above are integer orbit counts: the
division by the group order is carried out on integer
counts at chain level.
The caps are computed on the full two-generator torus
complex because the rank-one quotient does not
distinguish matching two lifts from matching two orbits.

\begin{proposition}[Integral excision]\label{prop:integral-excision}
With the end structures of Section~\ref{subsec:excision-structures},
form the composite $\overline{\mathsf E}\mathsf E$ with the right-placed
conversion at $Z'$. It is chain homotopic to the identity of $C_G(Z)$ up
to one sign. The maps $H(\mathsf E)$ and $H(\overline{\mathsf E})$ are
isomorphisms, and $\mathsf E$ and $\overline{\mathsf E}$ are chain
homotopy equivalences, after shifting degrees if necessary.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The one-dimensional gluing family identifies the
counts of Proposition~\ref{prop:capped} with the composite excision map. The same
argument for the other composite gives the reverse
comparison. There the cut is the separating torus cut
$T^2\times\gamma'$, so there is no trace and the matching
multiplicity is one. The lifting group has order eight, against
the capped group $\langle s_-,s_+\rangle$ of order four. On each cut
piece the meridional class of the removed cap exchanges
$\sigma\in\{g_1,g_2\}$ and moves one endpoint lift within its orbit,
so after summing over endpoint lifts the two matched choices
contribute equally. This gives a factor two, and the factors
balance: $2/8=1/4$. Consequently $H(\mathsf E)$ and
$H(\overline{\mathsf E})$ are isomorphisms. With the
end structures prescribed in Section~\ref{subsec:excision-structures},
$\mathsf E$ and $\overline{\mathsf E}$ have the same structure at
$Z$; at $Z'$ the composite is formed with the right-placed
conversion, as at the reference structures of the objects.
By \cite[Proposition~5.4]{KM-unknot} the composite is the map of $V$;
the hypothesis of that proposition holds because the class $c$ dual to
a component of $Z'$ lies in $\phi_V$. In terms of counts, by Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing}(a), the gluing multiplicity two at $Z'$ is absorbed by $|\phi_V|=8$; the identity holds at chain level, up to an integral chain homotopy, by Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing}(c). By the independence of the
almost-complex structure in Proposition~\ref{prop:gauge-functor}, the
map of $V$ may be computed for any almost-complex structure on $V$ as
in Lemma~\ref{lem:almost-complex}, after the end conversions, up to one
sign for all entries. The unconverted map of $V^\circ$ is even, so
$f=0$ and the capped factor is $(-1)^{|g|}/(uv)$; the capped cobordism $V'$ is a product on $Z$, so its map is the identity class up to one sign by Proposition~\ref{prop:gauge-functor}; hence the composite
is chain homotopic to the identity up to one sign, and in particular
it is $\pm1$ on every homogeneous class. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:acyclic}, the closed excision maps are
homotopy equivalences, after shifting degrees if necessary.
\end{proof}

The first assertion of Proposition~\ref{prop:integral-excision} is the only inverse-excision statement used in this paper; it enters through~\eqref{eq:excision-cycle}, on which Section~\ref{sec:cup} relies.
